## high: Compaction can drop messages appended while summary generation is in flight

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-b5dbc2a239-0134_a340107968
category: data-loss
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/agent/compaction (feat_library_b5dbc2a239)

evidence:
- backend/src/agent/compaction/engine.py:151-187

compact() snapshots current_messages, builds a replacement from that snapshot, then awaits the LLM summary before replacing the entire session history. If any user message, assistant chunk, tool call, or tool output is appended during the await, replace_with_stored_messages() overwrites history with the stale replacement list and silently loses the concurrent entries. This is especially risky because compaction is triggered near context limits, exactly when tool loops and retries may still be active.

recommendation:
Guard compaction with a session-history revision/checkpoint or lock. Before replacing history, verify that the current history is still the same snapshot that was compacted; if it changed, either rebuild/retry compaction against the new history or merge only the compacted prefix while preserving newly appended suffix entries.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for this feature, and the excerpt shows no concurrency or stale-history replacement coverage.

suggested regression test:
Add an async test where compaction starts, the fake LLM blocks, a new StoredMessage is appended to session.history, the LLM returns, and the final history is asserted to preserve the appended message or abort/retry instead of overwriting it.

minimum fix scope:
CompactionEngine.compact history replacement path plus the session/history API needed to expose an atomic revision check or replacement lock.

## high: Mid-chunk code fences leak code text to TTS

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-6a38404de9-50f4_b63c78a42f
category: bug
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/api/processing/tts (feat_library_6a38404de9)

evidence:
- backend/src/api/processing/tts/processor.py:123-143
- backend/src/api/processing/tts/processor.py:259-289

When text mode sees a code marker mid-chunk, it enters code suppression but recursively reprocesses post_marker including the opening ``` marker. The suppression branch then finds that marker at index 0 and treats it as the closing fence, immediately exits suppression, and processes the code body as normal text. For a chunk like 'text ```secret``` done', 'secret' is passed to TTS even though code blocks are intended to be filtered.

recommendation:
When entering code suppression mid-chunk, consume the opening marker before recursively processing the remainder, or make the suppression branch distinguish an opening fence from a closing fence based on state entered before the current chunk. Add coverage for code fences that start and end inside the same chunk and for fences that start mid-chunk.

test analysis:
No tests were included for this feature, and the shown implementation has no in-file assertions covering mid-chunk code fence transitions.

suggested regression test:
Add a processor unit test with a fake TTSManager/SpeechService asserting that 'hello ```print(secret)``` world' only sends 'hello ' and ' world' to TTS.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/api/processing/tts/processor.py code-marker transition logic plus focused TTSProcessor tests.

repro:
Call TTSProcessor._process_chunk with a ChunkEvent whose content is 'before ```secret``` after' after the processor is already in text mode. The manager receives 'before ', then 'secret', then ' after'.

## high: Provider credentials and OAuth tokens are persisted in renderer localStorage

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-8161046fcf-bd7e_772b23c15e
category: security
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/utils (feat_library_8161046fcf)

evidence:
- frontend/src/renderer/utils/configFilter.js:25-26
- frontend/src/renderer/utils/configStorage.js:15-31
- frontend/src/renderer/utils/configStorage.js:219-220
- frontend/src/renderer/utils/configStorage.js:285-286

The frontend allow-list includes provider_api_keys and provider_oauth, and configStorage normalizes access_token, refresh_token, and api_key values into the object written directly to localStorage. In an Electron renderer, localStorage is accessible to any renderer compromise or injected script, so long-lived API keys and OAuth refresh tokens become much easier to exfiltrate than if they were held behind a main-process or OS credential boundary.

recommendation:
Do not persist raw provider API keys or OAuth tokens in renderer localStorage. Store secrets in a main-process controlled credential store or OS keychain, expose only non-secret metadata such as connected/enabled/profile state to renderer config, and keep token access behind explicit IPC or SDK capability boundaries.

test analysis:
No tests are included for this feature, and a normal persistence round-trip test would likely assert the current insecure behavior unless it explicitly checks that secret fields are excluded from renderer storage.

suggested regression test:
Add a renderer config persistence test that saves provider_api_keys and provider_oauth with non-empty secret values, then asserts the serialized localStorage payload contains only safe metadata and no api_key, access_token, or refresh_token values.

minimum fix scope:
Change the frontend config filter/storage contract to exclude raw secret fields from localStorage and add a secure credential-storage path for those values.

## high: Replay failure leaves the UI on a truncated conversation

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-bfda34589c-ed9f_f2001fdbe7
category: data-loss
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/hooks#1 (feat_library_bfda34589c)

evidence:
- frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/hooks/useConversationReplayActions.js:100-105 (executeReplayAction)
- frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/hooks/useConversationReplayActions.js:107-131 (executeReplayAction)
- frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/hooks/useConversationReplayActions.js:164-178 (handleEditFromUser)
- frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/hooks/useConversationReplayActions.js:221-235 (handleTryAgainFromAssistant)

Both edit-and-resend and retry build a shortened replay conversation, immediately replace the visible messages with that replay state, and only then call the continuity service. If the service rejects, the catch path only clears isSending and never restores the original sourceMessages. A transient backend or IPC failure therefore permanently drops later messages from the active UI state even though the replay did not actually start.

recommendation:
Keep the original messages until the service accepts the replay, or restore sourceMessages in the catch block before clearing isSending. Also surface a local error message so the user can tell the replay failed.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for these replay hooks, and the failure branch currently has no assertion that the previous conversation is preserved on continuity-service rejection.

suggested regression test:
Add a hook/unit test that seeds messages, mocks retryTurn or editAndResend to reject, invokes the replay action, and asserts setMessages is either not called with the truncated replay state before success or is called again with the original source messages on failure.

minimum fix scope:
frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/hooks/useConversationReplayActions.js plus a focused frontend test for replay service failure rollback.

repro:
Trigger edit/resend or try-again on an older message, force DesktopConversationContinuityService.editAndResend or retryTurn to reject, and observe that messages after the replay point remain removed after the failure.

## high: Rewrite deletes the existing transcript before the replacement is durable

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-f3e886bb49-d302_afe0569007
category: data-loss
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/transcript#1 (feat_library_f3e886bb49)

evidence:
- frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/transcript/desktopConversationStore.ts:358-363 (rewriteTranscriptProjection)

rewriteTranscriptProjection removes the current conversation and only then appends the replacement events. If appendEvents rejects, the process exits, or IPC partially fails after deleteConversation succeeds, the previous transcript is already gone and the function never restores it. This makes a projection rewrite capable of permanently erasing a conversation on a transient write failure.

recommendation:
Make rewrite atomic at the storage boundary, or write the replacement revision first and only swap/delete the old revision after the append succeeds. If the IPC store cannot support transactions, add a two-phase replacement API in the owning store instead of composing delete plus append in the renderer.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for rewriteTranscriptProjection failure ordering or partial IPC failure behavior.

suggested regression test:
Mock createDesktopConversationStore so deleteConversation resolves and appendEvents rejects, then assert the rewrite path either does not delete first or invokes an atomic replacement API that preserves the old transcript on failure.

minimum fix scope:
Change rewriteTranscriptProjection and the sidecar/store API it calls enough to provide atomic replacement semantics or durable rollback.

## high: Runs API becomes unauthenticated when the key environment variable is unset

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-bcaf4a766a-6ab0_19455aafa4
category: security
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/api/routes/runs (feat_library_bcaf4a766a)

evidence:
- backend/src/api/routes/runs/support.py:43-59 (verify_runs_api_key)
- backend/src/api/routes/runs/router.py:44-49 (create_run)
- backend/src/api/routes/runs/router.py:124-129 (control_run)
- backend/src/api/routes/runs/router.py:144-149 (stop_all_runs)

Every mutating and read route depends on verify_runs_api_key, but verify_runs_api_key returns successfully when neither WINDIE_RUNS_API_KEY nor WINDIE_DEMO_API_KEY is configured. That turns missing configuration into an allow-all mode for creating runs, polling worker assignments, ingesting events, controlling runs, and stopping all runs. For hosted VM run/control endpoints, a fail-open auth dependency is a concrete permission gap and makes deployment misconfiguration dangerous.

recommendation:
Fail closed by default: require a configured runs API key for these routes, or gate unauthenticated mode behind an explicit local-development flag that cannot be enabled accidentally in hosted deployments. Add startup/config validation if the API is enabled but no key is configured.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for this feature, and the excerpts show no test coverage asserting the no-env-key behavior.

suggested regression test:
Add route-level tests that clear WINDIE_RUNS_API_KEY and WINDIE_DEMO_API_KEY and assert protected endpoints return an auth/config error, then set a key and assert missing or wrong x-windie-runs-key is rejected while the correct key succeeds.

minimum fix scope:
support.py auth helper plus focused route tests for at least one read endpoint and one mutating endpoint.

## high: Workspace permission can be granted from an arbitrary IPC path without user selection

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-fe7aecfc70-a388_cc054f2466
category: security
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/main/:permission (feat_library_fe7aecfc70)

evidence:
- frontend/src/main/permission_ipc_runtime.cjs:135-151
- frontend/src/main/permission_ipc_runtime.cjs:153-156
- frontend/src/main/permission_service_workspace.cjs:12-52

The `set-active-workspace` IPC handler accepts a renderer-provided string, stores it as `filesystem_workspace_access` with `granted: true`, and then emits the updated permission status. The later probe only checks that the selected path exists, not that it came from the OS folder picker or any trusted conversation binding. A compromised renderer or unintended caller can therefore grant workspace access to any existing local path without the explicit `showOpenDialog` flow used by `requestFilesystemWorkspaceAccessPermission`.

recommendation:
Restrict this handler to a trusted main-process conversation binding source, or require the same picker/allowlist validation as `requestFilesystemWorkspaceAccessPermission`. At minimum, validate sender/context and avoid writing `granted: true` for arbitrary renderer-supplied paths.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for permission IPC caller trust, workspace path provenance, or bypassing the workspace picker.

suggested regression test:
Add an IPC/runtime unit test that calls `set-active-workspace` with a raw renderer path and asserts it does not grant workspace access unless the call is marked as a trusted conversation binding or passes an approved picker result.

minimum fix scope:
Change `set-active-workspace` permission writes and add focused tests for workspace permission provenance.

repro:
Invoke `set-active-workspace` with an existing sensitive directory such as a home-directory subpath; the store is updated as granted and `runPermissionProbe('filesystem_workspace_access')` will return granted if the path exists.

## high: Artifact endpoints do not enforce authenticated access

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-57e7672bcd-aec7_749a6ab0e5
category: security
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/api/routes/artifacts (feat_library_57e7672bcd)

evidence:
- backend/src/api/routes/artifacts/router.py:23-35 (upload_artifact)
- backend/src/api/routes/artifacts/router.py:47-59 (get_artifact)

Both upload and fetch routes call get_current_authenticated_install_identity() but explicitly continue when it returns None, passing owner_user_id=None into storage operations. For an API described as handling large artifacts such as screenshots, this makes the route layer accept anonymous uploads and anonymous retrieval attempts instead of requiring an authenticated install identity before touching artifact storage. If the backend is reachable beyond a trusted local loopback, this is a permission gap for sensitive screenshot artifacts and can also allow unauthenticated storage abuse.

recommendation:
Require an authenticated install identity for artifact upload and retrieval, returning 401/403 before save_upload or resolve_path_for_owner when identity is missing. If anonymous artifacts are intentionally supported, make that an explicit route contract and isolate them from authenticated artifact namespaces with tests.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for this feature, and there is no visible route-level assertion that missing identity is rejected.

suggested regression test:
Add FastAPI route tests that mock get_current_authenticated_install_identity() returning None and assert POST /api/artifacts/ and GET /api/artifacts/{artifact_id} return 401 or 403 without calling ArtifactStore.save_upload or ArtifactStore.resolve_path_for_owner.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/api/routes/artifacts/router.py plus focused backend route tests.

## high: Passwordless sudo grant is unbounded after one OS approval

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-d66dd70414-cf58_b6c6cc7e45
category: security
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node package windieos (feat_library_d66dd70414)

evidence:
- frontend/src/main/agent_sudo_access_handler.cjs:3
- frontend/src/main/agent_sudo_access_handler.cjs:28-37
- frontend/src/main/agent_sudo_access_handler.cjs:163-179

Enabling agent sudo access writes a sudoers rule that grants the current user `NOPASSWD: ALL`, which removes password prompts for every sudo command on the machine, not just WindieOS-controlled commands. A single successful pkexec approval therefore permanently broadens privilege for the whole user session/system until disabled, so any later local process running as that user can invoke arbitrary sudo commands without authentication.

recommendation:
Replace the blanket sudoers rule with the narrowest command allowlist needed by WindieOS, or avoid persistent sudoers mutation and use per-operation OS authorization. If broad access is intentionally required, gate it behind explicit high-risk copy and add a verification path that reports the exact sudoers scope.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for the generated sudoers content or for limiting the privilege scope of the rule.

suggested regression test:
Add a unit test for the enable-script builder that asserts the sudoers rule does not contain `NOPASSWD: ALL` and only grants approved WindieOS command paths.

minimum fix scope:
frontend/src/main/agent_sudo_access_handler.cjs and focused tests around generated sudoers rules and disable behavior.

repro:
On Linux, enable passwordless sudo through this handler. After it returns success, run any unrelated `sudo` command from the same user account; it will no longer require a password because the sudoers rule applies to `ALL` commands.

## high: Query-plan debug route exposes prompt construction without an explicit authorization gate

id: fnd_sig-feat-route-5546b12cae-860668_3b9a8b6f17
category: security
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: FastAPI route POST /api/sdk/query-plan (feat_route_5546b12cae)

evidence:
- backend/src/api/routes/sdk/router.py:503-526 (sdk_debug_query_plan)
- backend/src/api/routes/sdk/router.py:527-538 (sdk_debug_query_plan)

The POST /api/sdk/query-plan handler accepts network-provided debug inputs, including user_id, workspace_path, agent_definition, and conversation_ref, then returns the resolved config, query message, transparency events, system prompt, prompt messages, canonical and provider tool schemas, and full user message. In the shown route there is no explicit auth, permission, caller ownership, or debug-mode dependency before resolving another requested user/config/conversation context. If this router is reachable outside a trusted local/debug channel, a caller could inspect prompt assembly and potentially sensitive conversation or workspace-derived prompt material for arbitrary payload-selected contexts.

recommendation:
Gate this route behind the same authenticated/debug-only authorization boundary used for privileged SDK diagnostics, and validate that payload-selected user_id, workspace_path, and conversation_ref are owned by or permitted for the caller before building or returning the plan.

test analysis:
No linked tests were provided for this feature, and the route excerpt does not show any test-enforced authorization or ownership invariant for query-plan payloads.

suggested regression test:
Add a FastAPI route test that unauthenticated or unauthorized callers cannot call POST /api/sdk/query-plan, and that an authenticated caller cannot request another user's conversation_ref or workspace context.

minimum fix scope:
Add route-level or router-level auth/debug dependency plus ownership validation for user_id/conversation_ref/workspace_path before build_query_plan is invoked.

## high: Response overlay renders prebuilt markdown HTML without a local sanitization boundary

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-d8cdfe998d-8988_220f4b3aef
category: security
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/components (feat_library_d8cdfe998d)

evidence:
- frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/components/ChatBoxResponse.jsx:31-35
- frontend/package.json:44-50

Default response entries are injected with dangerouslySetInnerHTML using entry.markdownHtml. The excerpt does not show a local DOMPurify/sanitization step or an invariant check before injection, even though this renderer has DOMPurify available. If markdownHtml is derived from assistant/tool/search content, a malicious model output or tool result could execute HTML/script-like payloads in the Electron renderer context. This is higher impact than ordinary web XSS because the renderer participates in desktop app IPC and local workflows.

recommendation:
Sanitize markdownHtml at the rendering boundary, or change the response overlay contract so it only accepts a branded/sanitized HTML value created by a single sanitizer module. Add tests that prove scriptable HTML, event handlers, javascript: URLs, and unsafe SVG/MathML payloads are removed before rendering.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for ChatBoxResponse or the response overlay HTML contract, and the component currently accepts markdownHtml directly.

suggested regression test:
Add a renderer/unit test for ChatBoxResponse or the overlay view model that renders a response entry containing unsafe HTML and asserts no unsafe attributes/elements survive in the DOM.

minimum fix scope:
ChatBoxResponse rendering boundary plus the response-overlay markdown/sanitizer contract used to create entry.markdownHtml.

## high: Screenshot materialization trusts and deletes arbitrary returned paths

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-47df125989-f88a_fafffd7241
category: security
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/main/:local (feat_library_47df125989)

evidence:
- frontend/src/main/local_backend_bridge_execute_tool_runtime.cjs:119-141
- frontend/src/main/local_backend_bridge_screenshot_attachment.cjs:106-160

Every tool result, including local MCP tool results, is passed through screenshot materialization. If the result contains data.screenshot_path, the main process reads that path, uploads or inlines its contents, and then unlinks it in finally. There is no check that the path came from the screenshot tool, is inside an expected temporary screenshot directory, or was created by the sidecar for this request. A malicious or buggy local tool/MCP result can therefore exfiltrate and delete an arbitrary user-readable file by returning it as screenshot_path.

recommendation:
Only materialize screenshot attachments for the screenshot tool, and validate screenshot_path against an owned temporary screenshot directory or an opaque sidecar-issued artifact handle before reading or deleting it. Do not unlink paths that fail validation.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for screenshot attachment materialization, and the current code has no assertions around path ownership or tool-name gating.

suggested regression test:
Add a main-process unit test that executes a non-screenshot local MCP tool returning data.screenshot_path outside the screenshot temp directory and asserts the file is neither read nor deleted and no screenshot payload is attached.

minimum fix scope:
Gate materializeScreenshotAttachment by toolName in executeTool and add path ownership validation before readFile/unlink in local_backend_bridge_screenshot_attachment.cjs.

## high: SDK OCR find-text route is exposed without any route-level authentication or authorization dependency

id: fnd_sig-feat-route-bc1a119a3b-3e2c23_1fc677e56c
category: security
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: FastAPI route POST /api/sdk/ocr/find-text (feat_route_bc1a119a3b)

evidence:
- backend/src/api/routes/sdk/router.py:67 (router)
- backend/src/api/routes/sdk/router.py:163-170 (sdk_ocr_find_text)

The SDK router is created with only a prefix and tags, and the POST /api/sdk/ocr/find-text handler accepts a caller-provided image/text request and immediately resolves the image source and runs OCR. In the shown route there is no FastAPI Depends, session/user validation, capability check, or request-scoped authorization before doing backend work. Because this feature crosses network, user-input, serialization, and auth trust boundaries, an exposed unauthenticated route could let any network caller submit images for OCR and receive extracted match metadata.

recommendation:
Require the same authenticated SDK/session dependency used for protected backend APIs before resolving the image source or running OCR. If this endpoint is intentionally local-only, enforce that invariant in the route/dependency layer and document it in the API contract.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for this feature, and the excerpt shows no route-level dependency that a test could exercise for unauthorized requests.

suggested regression test:
Add a FastAPI route test for POST /api/sdk/ocr/find-text that asserts unauthenticated or unauthorized requests are rejected before OCR/image resolution is invoked, plus an authenticated success path.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/api/routes/sdk/router.py and the focused SDK OCR route tests

## high: Session package imports a module that is not part of the session source group

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-67d1b5afa7-0456_a80b718689
category: build-release
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/agent/session (feat_library_67d1b5afa7)

evidence:
- backend/src/agent/session/__init__.py:3-8
- backend/src/agent/session/manager.py:8-14

The package initializer and manager both import backend.src.agent.session.session.AgentSession, but the reviewed source group contains no backend/src/agent/session/session.py and the prompt states there are no omitted files. If that module is genuinely absent, importing backend.src.agent.session or backend.src.agent.session.manager will fail at import time, taking down session management before any runtime behavior can execute.

recommendation:
Restore or move the AgentSession implementation into backend/src/agent/session/session.py, or update these imports to the current AgentSession module and include a regression import test for the package and manager.

test analysis:
The included test imports ActiveQueryTracker directly from its module and never imports backend.src.agent.session or SessionManager, so it would not exercise the broken package-level import path.

suggested regression test:
Add a backend import smoke test that imports `backend.src.agent.session`, `SessionManager`, and `AgentSession` from the public package surface.

minimum fix scope:
Fix the AgentSession import target in __init__.py and manager.py, or add the missing session.py module if it was unintentionally dropped.

repro:
Run `python -c "import backend.src.agent.session"` in a checkout where backend/src/agent/session/session.py is absent; the import should raise ModuleNotFoundError.

## high: System prompt debug route is exposed without route-level authorization

id: fnd_sig-feat-route-7823aa9370-602e24_be04a76732
category: security
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: FastAPI route GET /api/sdk/system-prompt (feat_route_7823aa9370)

evidence:
- backend/src/api/routes/sdk/router.py:444-465 (sdk_debug_system_prompt)

The GET /api/sdk/system-prompt handler accepts network query parameters, including user_id/model selection, builds the effective debug config, and returns the raw system_prompt. The route signature has no visible authentication, authorization, or debug-mode guard dependency, so any caller able to reach this SDK router could retrieve internal prompt instructions and potentially request another user's effective prompt by supplying user_id. If this router is mounted only behind a global auth/debug gate elsewhere, that would mitigate the issue, but no such protection is present in the included owned file.

recommendation:
Require an explicit authorization/debug capability check for this route before building or returning the prompt. Bind user_id to the authenticated principal or remove arbitrary user_id selection unless the caller has an admin/debug permission. Consider returning redacted prompt metadata by default and raw prompt text only under a deliberate debug flag.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for this feature, and the excerpt does not show any route-level test asserting unauthorized callers are rejected or that user_id is constrained to the caller.

suggested regression test:
Add FastAPI route tests for GET /api/sdk/system-prompt covering unauthenticated rejection, non-admin cross-user user_id rejection, and authorized debug/admin access returning the expected response shape.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/api/routes/sdk/router.py route dependency or shared SDK debug-route guard used by this handler

## high: Unauthenticated calls can select the user context from the request body

id: fnd_sig-feat-route-5114a60cce-5f2c84_4e28c9d291
category: security
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: FastAPI route POST /api/semantic/title (feat_route_5114a60cce)

evidence:
- backend/src/api/routes/memory/semantic/router.py:149-166 (generate_conversation_title)

The route resolves the user id from authenticated context when present, but falls back to request.user_id when no authenticated identity exists, then uses that resolved id to generate the title with the shared container/session manager. For a network route with an auth trust boundary, this makes the authorization boundary optional at the handler level: a caller without identity can supply another user's id and potentially use that user's model/session configuration for title generation. The excerpt does not show a route-level dependency that rejects missing identity, so the handler itself does not enforce the boundary.

recommendation:
Require an authenticated install identity for this route, or explicitly gate the unauthenticated local path behind a separate trusted transport/dependency. Do not use request.user_id as an authorization fallback for network requests; at most treat it as advisory and verify it matches the authenticated identity.

test analysis:
No linked tests were provided for this route, and the excerpt has no evidence of a test covering missing authenticated identity or cross-user request.user_id spoofing.

suggested regression test:
Add a route-level test for POST /api/semantic/title that verifies a request without authenticated identity is rejected, and a request whose body user_id differs from the authenticated identity cannot cause service.generate_title to receive the body user_id.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/api/routes/memory/semantic/router.py and focused backend route tests

## high: Unauthenticated requests can act as any user_id

id: fnd_sig-feat-route-a7697140e9-81e197_bdc8073b61
category: security
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: FastAPI route POST /api/semantic/summarize (feat_route_a7697140e9)

evidence:
- backend/src/api/routes/memory/semantic/router.py:96-104 (summarize_conversations)
- backend/src/api/routes/memory/semantic/router.py:112-117 (summarize_conversations)

The route accepts network input and does not declare an authentication dependency in its signature or decorator. It reads the authenticated install identity from ambient context, but if no identity is present it falls back to request.user_id and passes that resolved value into the summarization service. That means an unauthenticated or context-missing request can select an arbitrary user_id for model/session-backed summarization, which is an authorization bypass if the router is not protected at a higher level. The excerpt provides no linked tests or route-level guard showing this fallback is intentionally restricted.

recommendation:
Require authenticated identity for this endpoint, or explicitly reject requests when identity is absent. If a legacy unauthenticated mode is still required, constrain it to a non-user-scoped local identity and document/test the boundary.

test analysis:
No tests were included for this feature, and the shown code has no assertion that unauthenticated requests are rejected or that request.user_id cannot impersonate another user.

suggested regression test:
Add a FastAPI route test that calls POST /api/semantic/summarize without an authenticated identity and asserts 401/403, plus a test where authenticated identity.user_id differs from request.user_id and verifies the authenticated user is used.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/api/routes/memory/semantic/router.py authentication handling for summarize_conversations, with focused route tests.

## high: Unauthenticated semantic requests can choose an arbitrary user_id

Both semantic routes use the authenticated identity when present, but silently fall back to the caller-supplied request.user_id when no identity is available. The models only validate the shape of user_id, not authorization. recommendation:
Require an authenticated install identity for these routes, or enforce an explicit local-only trust boundary before accepting request.user_id. backend/src/api/routes/memory/semantic/router.py and focused route/auth tests.

## medium: Artifact image failures are cached permanently

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-4caa9ecdef-a229_af8de9602b
category: bug
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/utils/message (feat_library_4caa9ecdef)

evidence:
- frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/utils/message/useResolvedMessageScreenshots.js:27-42 (resolveArtifactAttachmentSrc)

The artifact resolver stores the IPC promise in a module-level cache before it resolves, and the catch path converts any failure to null. Because the null-producing promise remains cached, a transient FETCH_ARTIFACT_IMAGE failure, permission race, backend restart, or artifact service warmup issue makes that artifact image unrecoverable for the lifetime of the renderer session. Future renders reuse the cached null result and never retry.

recommendation:
Evict the cache entry on rejection or null result, or cache only successful non-empty data URLs. If deduping in-flight requests is still desired, keep the pending promise while in flight and delete it when it resolves to null or rejects.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for artifact screenshot resolution or retry behavior, and the package manifest only shows generic Jest commands.

suggested regression test:
Add a hook/unit test that mocks IpcBridge.invoke to reject once and then return a valid dataUrl, verifying that a later render retries and displays the image.

minimum fix scope:
Update resolveArtifactAttachmentSrc cache handling and add focused frontend coverage for retry after failure.

repro:
Render a message whose screenshot requires artifact resolution, make FETCH_ARTIFACT_IMAGE reject once for that artifact key, then render the same message again after the service is available. The second render still resolves to no source because the rejected result was cached as null.

## medium: Async cache coalescing shares asyncio.Event across event loops

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-2fdb111c39-1a17_58e49e6429
category: concurrency
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/core/infrastructure (feat_library_2fdb111c39)

evidence:
- backend/src/core/infrastructure/cache_store.py:47-49 (Cache.__init__)
- backend/src/core/infrastructure/cache_store.py:77-84 (Cache._new_async_event)
- backend/src/core/infrastructure/cache_store.py:213-236 (Cache.get_or_compute_async)

The cache is documented as thread-safe, and the async coalescing map is stored on the shared Cache instance, but it stores raw asyncio.Event objects. Those events are loop-affine/non-thread-safe synchronization primitives, so concurrent get_or_compute_async calls for the same key from different event loops can cause waiters to await an event created for another loop or miss correct wakeup semantics. This can raise runtime errors or leave callers blocked in multi-loop backend/test contexts.

recommendation:
Do not share asyncio.Event instances across loops. Scope async in-flight coordination by both key and running loop, or replace it with loop-safe coordination such as per-loop futures plus thread-safe completion notification. Keep the threading lock only for registry mutation, not as a substitute for asyncio primitive safety.

test analysis:
The included tests only cover CacheEntry and CacheManager key/stat behavior; they never exercise Cache.get_or_compute_async, cross-loop callers, or async in-flight coalescing.

suggested regression test:
Add a backend test that calls Cache.get_or_compute_async for the same key from two separate threads with separate event loops and asserts both callers complete and compute_func runs only according to the intended per-loop/coalescing contract.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/core/infrastructure/cache_store.py plus focused async cache tests.

repro:
Create one Cache instance, start get_or_compute_async for the same key concurrently from two different threads each running its own event loop, and have the second caller hit the existing _computing_async event at lines 214-220 before awaiting it at line 236.

## medium: Atomic bundle results are not cleaned up if history processing fails

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-f977796e67-3439_10d9b86cb6
category: performance
confidence: high
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/agent/tools/processing (feat_library_f977796e67)

evidence:
- backend/src/agent/tools/processing/processor.py:80-134 (ToolResultProcessor.process)
- backend/src/agent/tools/processing/processor.py:145-178 (ToolResultProcessor.process)

The non-bundled path extracts request ids before processing and performs cleanup in a finally block, so transform or history commit failures still release stored results and resolved tool calls. The atomic bundle path instead removes the stored bundle only after transform and commit both succeed, then returns. If BundleResultFormatter, transform, or history_committer.commit raises, session.remove_bundle_result(bundle_id) is skipped and the stored bundle remains in session storage for the lifetime of the session or until some unrelated cleanup handles it. The surrounding comments already identify this class of failure as a memory leak in the individual-result path, but the same protection is missing for bundled results.

recommendation:
Wrap the atomic bundle transform/commit section in try/finally once the bundle_id has been found, and call session.remove_bundle_result(bundle_id) in the finally block. Keep the successful return after cleanup, and preserve the existing behavior for missing bundle results falling through to individual processing.

test analysis:
The included tests only cover canonical tool output projection and format_for_history behavior. They do not exercise ToolResultProcessor.process, atomic bundle storage, or failure cleanup paths.

suggested regression test:
Add a backend unit test for ToolResultProcessor.process with is_atomic_bundle_from_results true, a session returning a bundle result, and a committer that raises; assert the exception propagates and remove_bundle_result is still called exactly once.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/agent/tools/processing/processor.py plus a focused backend processor cleanup test.

repro:
Create an atomic bundle with a stored bundle result, inject a HistoryCommitter whose commit method raises, call ToolResultProcessor.process, then assert session.remove_bundle_result(bundle_id) was called. It will not be called on the current atomic-bundle path.

## medium: Audit sanitization can still retain or materialize large parameter payloads

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-0542387a45-91ec_a1b4b7b8bc
category: security
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/core/security (feat_library_0542387a45)

evidence:
- backend/src/core/security/policy.py:44-70 (ToolExecutionAudit)
- backend/src/core/security/policy.py:107-137 (ToolExecutionAudit._sanitize_parameters)

The audit entry explicitly promises bounded memory use for large parameters, but excluded keys call len(str(value)), which can materialize an entire large payload just to report its size. Non-string, non-dict, non-list values are also stored as-is, so large bytes, bytearrays, tuples, custom containers, or objects under non-excluded keys can remain in the capped deque. A capped entry count does not prevent memory exhaustion if each retained entry can contain large objects.

recommendation:
Avoid stringifying excluded values for size reporting unless the value has a cheap length, and sanitize or summarize all non-scalar values instead of preserving them by reference. Treat bytes-like values and generic containers as bounded summaries, and only keep primitive scalar values directly.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for audit sanitization, and the excerpted implementation contains comments for prior recursion and memory-DoS fixes without coverage evidence for bytes-like or generic object payloads.

suggested regression test:
Add tests that create ToolExecutionAudit entries with very large bytes/bytearray values, excluded-key objects whose __str__ is expensive or huge, nested containers, and arbitrary objects, then assert the sanitized parameters contain bounded summaries and do not retain the original payload object.

minimum fix scope:
ToolExecutionAudit._sanitize_parameters and focused backend security-policy tests.

## medium: Bundle screenshot attachment can miss successful actions after skipped steps

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-158eb1ddef-92d3_5a10c41b7b
category: bug
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: open
feature: Node source packages/windie-sdk-js/src/tools (feat_library_158eb1ddef)

evidence:
- packages/windie-sdk-js/src/tools/ToolExecutionCoordinator.ts:243-260 (bundleContainsCaptureWorthyTool/findBundleScreenshotFromExplicitStep)
- packages/windie-sdk-js/src/tools/ToolExecutionCoordinator.ts:411-445 (executeBundle)
- packages/windie-sdk-js/src/tools/ToolExecutionCoordinator.cjs:241-265 (bundleContainsCaptureWorthyTool/findBundleScreenshotFromExplicitStep)
- packages/windie-sdk-js/src/tools/ToolExecutionCoordinator.cjs:553-579 (routeToolBundleToLocalRuntime)

Bundle execution skips invalid or nameless tool entries while building stepResults, but later screenshot attachment assumes stepResults has the same indexes as the original tools array. If an invalid entry appears before a successful capture-worthy tool, the successful step shifts to an earlier index and bundleContainsCaptureWorthyTool can read undefined for that tool index, so no post-action screenshot is captured. The same index mismatch can prevent reuse of an explicit screenshot step.

recommendation:
Preserve the original tool index in each step result or build an aligned list that includes skipped steps, then use that stable mapping when deciding capture-worthy and explicit screenshot behavior.

test analysis:
No linked tests were provided for this feature, and the shown code has no assertion covering malformed bundle entries before screenshot-producing or capture-worthy steps.

suggested regression test:
Add a bundle coordinator test where an invalid first tool is followed by a successful click and assert that a post-action screenshot is requested and attached. Add the same shape for an explicit screenshot step after a skipped entry.

minimum fix scope:
Update bundle step result construction and screenshot attachment in both the TypeScript source and the CJS runtime copy, plus focused SDK tests for shifted bundle indexes.

repro:
Pass a tool bundle with tools like [{}, { name: 'click', args: { x: 1, y: 1 } }] where the click executes successfully. stepResults contains one ok click result at index 0, but bundleContainsCaptureWorthyTool checks the click at tools index 1 against stepResults[1], so it returns false and skips capture.

## medium: Concurrent file-store appends can overwrite each other

id: fnd_sig-feat-service-da2cdbf6a3-79f6_ad2e6d88c5
category: data-loss
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: open
feature: Node source packages/windie-sdk-js/src/stores (feat_service_da2cdbf6a3)

evidence:
- packages/windie-sdk-js/src/stores/FileConversationStore.ts:154-173 (FileConversationStore.appendEvents)
- packages/windie-sdk-js/src/stores/FileConversationStore.ts:312-318 (FileConversationStore.writeConversation)

FileConversationStore.appendEvents performs an unlocked read-modify-write per conversation. Two overlapping appendEvents calls for the same conversation can both read the same stored event list, merge their own unique events, and then each rename a complete JSON file over the target. The later rename wins and silently drops the other append, even though the temporary-file rename only makes each individual write atomic.

recommendation:
Serialize writes per conversationRef in FileConversationStore, or add an optimistic revision check/retry loop that rereads and remerges if the target changed between read and rename.

test analysis:
No linked tests were provided for this feature, and the included code has no evidence of a concurrency test around overlapping file-backed appends.

suggested regression test:
Add a FileConversationStore test using a controllable fs mock that pauses both appendEvent calls after readConversation, then releases writes in reverse order and asserts both unique events are retained.

minimum fix scope:
FileConversationStore append/write path for same-conversation writes.

repro:
Start with an empty file-backed conversation, issue two appendEvent calls for the same conversationRef concurrently with different eventIds, and observe that the final JSON can contain only one of the two events depending on write ordering.

## medium: Concurrent screenshot captures can clear the active capture state too early

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-7647eac87f-f39d_795729c26f
category: concurrency
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/services (feat_library_7647eac87f)

evidence:
- frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/services/ScreenshotAttachmentPipeline.ts:181-186 (captureScreenshotAttachment)
- frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/services/ScreenshotAttachmentPipeline.ts:220-230 (captureScreenshotAttachment)

Each capture emits a global `windie:screenshot-capture` active=true event at start and active=false in its own finally block. With two overlapping captures, the first one to finish emits active=false even though the second capture is still running. Any listener that uses this event to gate UI state, capture masking, or user feedback will observe the capture as inactive during an active capture window.

recommendation:
Track active screenshot captures with a shared counter or capture-id set and emit `active: false` only when the last outstanding capture completes. Keep the event payload compatible, optionally adding the count/capture id for diagnostics.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for this source group, and the excerpt shows no scenario coverage for overlapping capture calls or global capture event ordering.

suggested regression test:
Add a unit test that stubs the IPC screenshot call with two deferred promises, starts two captures, resolves the first, and asserts no `active:false` event is dispatched until the second resolves.

minimum fix scope:
ScreenshotAttachmentPipeline.ts plus a focused frontend test for capture event reference-counting.

repro:
Start two `captureScreenshotAttachment()` calls without awaiting the first. Let the shorter one finish first; it dispatches `active: false` while the longer one is still between its active=true dispatch and its finally block.

## medium: CPU feature probe is misclassified as a user-facing CLI command

id: fnd_sig-feat-cli-command-10fd2d832c-_18b2265653
category: build-release
confidence: high
triage: risk
status: false-positive
feature: C binary cpu_avx512cd (feat_cli-command_10fd2d832c)

evidence:
- frontend/python-runtime/lib/python3.11/site-packages/numpy/distutils/checks/cpu_avx512cd.c:16-19 (main)

The detected entrypoint is a NumPy distutils CPU capability probe, not a real WindieOS command. Its main() immediately executes an AVX512CD intrinsic and reads a 512-bit vector from argv memory, with no command contract, argument validation, help path, or runtime CPU guard. If clawpatch treats this as an executable CLI feature named cpu_avx512cd, invoking it on ordinary user input or an unsupported CPU can crash or fail unpredictably, and packaging/release logic may surface vendored build-check sources as product commands.

recommendation:
Exclude vendored runtime paths such as frontend/python-runtime/lib/python3.11/site-packages/numpy/distutils/checks from CLI-command feature discovery, or classify these files as build probes rather than product entrypoints.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for this feature, and the file itself contains no assertions or command-level behavior to validate the claimed CLI contract.

## medium: Disabled math still rewrites LaTeX delimiters

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-dab12c8b1a-80df_8420248f2d
category: api-contract

- frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/markdown.ts:396-397 (toSanitizedMarkdownHtml)

resolveLlmOutputContract computes mathEnabled from options.enableMath, but normalizeLatexMathDelimiters runs unconditionally. That means callers that pass enableMath: false still get \(...\) rewritten to $...$ and \[...\] rewritten to $$...$$ before rendering. The renderer later honors enableMath by choosing the non-math Marked instance, so disabled math can surface as altered literal text rather than preserving the provider output.

recommendation:
Only call normalizeLatexMathDelimiters when mathEnabled is true, or remove enableMath from this contract if normalization is intentionally independent of math rendering.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for this feature, and the provided excerpts do not show coverage for enableMath=false behavior.

suggested regression test:
Add a renderer infrastructure test that calls resolveLlmOutputContract('Use \\(x+1\\)', { enableMath: false }) and asserts the markdown remains unchanged, then verifies enableMath=true still normalizes delimiters.

minimum fix scope:
Change resolveLlmOutputContract to gate delimiter normalization on mathEnabled and add focused tests for both enabled and disabled math paths.

## medium: Disabled web_search policy does not prevent direct Brave execution

- backend/src/tools/web_search/tool.py:526-555 (WebSearchTool.run)

The capability resolver returns None when web_search is disabled, but WebSearchTool.run treats any non-native mode, including None, as permission to resolve a Brave API key and execute the backend search. That means a disabled capability can still run if the tool is invoked directly, through a stale registry, or through another backend path that reaches run(). Exposure policy and execution policy are not enforced at the same boundary.

recommendation:
Make run() distinguish disabled/unavailable from backend-brave. Only execute Brave when resolve_web_search_execution_mode(config) returns "backend-brave", and return a policy/configuration error otherwise. test analysis:
No linked tests were included for disabled capability enforcement or direct tool invocation with a configured Brave key.

suggested regression test:
Add a backend unit test with disabled_capabilities containing web_search and BRAVE_SEARCH_API_KEY set; assert WebSearchTool.run() does not call _perform_request and returns a failure indicating web_search is disabled or unavailable.

minimum fix scope:
WebSearchTool.run execution-mode branching plus focused backend tests.

## medium: Disconnect during startup sends can bypass task/session cleanup

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-3eca321ef8-c3a9_a4d6cc5eec
category: bug
confidence: high

status: open

- backend/src/api/routes/websocket/router.py:139-199
- backend/src/api/routes/websocket/connection.py:222-233

The route increments the session connection count immediately after a successful handshake, but the cleanup finally block does not begin until line 139. The client-tool-manifest and remote-tool-catalog sends happen before that protected region. If either send raises because the client disconnected or the socket failed, cleanup_connection is skipped, so the per-user connection count is not decremented and end_session may never run for that connection. This can leak session state and leave subsequent cleanup decisions using a stale active-connection count.

recommendation:
Move the try/finally to cover all post-handshake session mutation and startup sends, or wrap the startup sends in the same protected connection lifecycle block after increment_connection_count.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for startup send failures or disconnects between handshake completion and receive-loop entry.

suggested regression test:
Add a websocket route test where SafeWebSocket.send_json raises after increment_connection_count, then assert cleanup_connection runs and session_manager.decrement_connection_count/end_session are called as appropriate.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/api/routes/websocket/router.py

## medium: Duplicate handler registration silently overwrites despite documented rejection

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-af30eb86f9-5ca9_286641dfad

The register docstring declares ValueError on an already registered message_type, but the implementation only logs a warning and overwrites the existing handler. That makes handler ownership order-dependent and can replace an existing route without a hard failure, which is especially risky for WebSocket message routing where duplicate message types should be caught during startup or test setup.

recommendation:
Either enforce the documented contract by raising ValueError on duplicate registration, or intentionally change the API to make replacement explicit with a separate replace/override path and update docs/tests accordingly.

## medium: Embedded JSON parsing bypasses configured nesting-depth validation

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-68324f407d-d970_e49ec81d1c
category: security
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/llm (feat_library_68324f407d)

evidence:
- backend/src/llm/parser_extraction.py:105-116 (JsonToolCallExtractor.parse_embedded_json)
- backend/src/llm/parser_extraction.py:154-157 (JsonToolCallExtractor._safe_json_loads)
- backend/src/llm/parser_extraction.py:218-235 (JsonToolCallExtractor._validate_json_depth)

The pure-JSON path calls _safe_json_loads, which validates max_json_nesting_depth after json.loads. The embedded path decodes objects with JSONDecoder.raw_decode and immediately extracts tool calls without calling _validate_json_depth. That creates inconsistent trust-boundary enforcement: a deeply nested tool-call args payload is rejected when it is the whole response but can be accepted when wrapped in surrounding text.

recommendation:
Run _validate_json_depth(parsed, self.limits.max_json_nesting_depth) after raw_decode and before schema extraction in parse_embedded_json, converting RecursionError to ParseValidationError consistently with _safe_json_loads.

test analysis:
The included tests exercise timeout, extraction, removal, max_response_size, max_json_size for trailing text, and validator constraints, but none assert that embedded JSON observes max_json_nesting_depth.

suggested regression test:
Add a parser_extraction test that configures max_json_nesting_depth to a small value and asserts an embedded over-depth functionCall raises ParseValidationError.

minimum fix scope:
Add depth validation to the embedded JSON path and focused regression coverage.

repro:
Set a low max_json_nesting_depth and parse an embedded response containing `prefix {"functionCall":{"name":"read_file","args":{"a":{"b":{"c":1}}}}} suffix`. The embedded raw_decode path reaches schema extraction and validation without the depth check used by _safe_json_loads.

## medium: Embedded oversized tool-call JSON is silently ignored instead of rejected

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-68324f407d-980e_7f6cd59934
category: security
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/llm (feat_library_68324f407d)

evidence:
- backend/src/llm/parser_extraction.py:112-114 (JsonToolCallExtractor.parse_embedded_json)
- backend/src/llm/parser_extraction.py:36-48 (JsonToolCallExtractor.parse_json_response)
- backend/src/llm/parser.py:142-150 (ResponseParser.parse_response)

The parser advertises max JSON size enforcement as part of the LLM response trust boundary, and pure JSON responses over max_json_size raise InputSizeLimitError. Embedded JSON takes a different path: when an embedded JSON object exceeds max_json_size, parse_embedded_json just advances the scan position and continues. A model can therefore include an oversized tool-call-shaped JSON object inside surrounding text and have it treated as conversational content, bypassing the hard-error behavior and metrics used for pure JSON.

recommendation:
Make embedded JSON size violations raise InputSizeLimitError and record the size violation when the decoded JSON object exceeds max_json_size, at least when the object has the configured tool-call root key. If deliberately ignoring non-tool JSON is required, inspect only enough to distinguish tool-call-shaped payloads before deciding whether to raise.

test analysis:
The included extraction tests cover the max_response_size scan boundary and a small valid JSON object followed by large trailing text, but there is no test for an embedded tool-call JSON object whose own size exceeds max_json_size.

suggested regression test:
Add a parser_extraction test with low max_json_size and an embedded functionCall object larger than the limit; assert InputSizeLimitError and a size-violation metric rather than an empty tool call list.

minimum fix scope:
Update JsonToolCallExtractor.parse_embedded_json oversized-object handling and add focused parser_extraction coverage.

repro:
Configure max_json_size smaller than an embedded JSON object that contains a functionCall, then parse a response like `prefix {"functionCall":{"name":"read_file","args":{"payload":"...large..."}}} suffix`. The embedded path skips the object instead of raising the same size-limit error as parse_json_response.

## medium: Error helper can send unsanitized raw messages despite the canonical security contract

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-af30eb86f9-17f2_8a5a5db9d8
category: security
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug

- backend/src/api/infrastructure/errors.py:108-121
- backend/src/api/infrastructure/errors.py:123-138

The module-level contract says all client-facing error messages are sanitized to prevent leakage, but send_error_response only sanitizes when an exception object is supplied. If callers pass a raw exception string, file path, provider response, or internal state as the message argument without also passing exception, that content is placed directly in the transport payload. Because the same doc says all handlers must use this utility, this helper is the intended enforcement point and currently does not enforce the stated security invariant.

recommendation:
Make send_error_response enforce sanitization for all error responses, for example by requiring exception for internal failures and adding an explicit safe/user_facing flag or typed user-facing error path for validation messages. Avoid sending arbitrary message strings as-is by default.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for this feature, and the excerpt does not show a regression test asserting that raw internal error messages are blocked when exception is omitted.

suggested regression test:
Add a test that calls send_error_response with a raw internal-looking message and no exception, then asserts the emitted payload contains only an approved safe message unless the call explicitly marks the message as user-facing.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/api/infrastructure/errors.py plus focused tests for error payload sanitization behavior.

## medium: Explicit userId clears are ignored in session state

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-f3e886bb49-7692_72fb290fba
category: bug
confidence: high

The update API accepts userId?: string | null, and the sync payload parser preserves an explicit null userId. However update computes nextUserId with userId || currentUserId and only assigns when nextUserId is truthy, so a caller cannot clear the stored userId. A session switch or logout-style sync that sends userId: null will keep using the prior userId, which can attach later transcript operations to the wrong user.

recommendation:
Treat undefined as 'leave unchanged' and null as 'clear'. For example, compute nextUserId with userId === undefined ? currentUserId : userId and assign currentUserId directly.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for session sync null semantics or clearing a previously loaded userId.

suggested regression test:
Create a transcript session state with an initial stored userId, call update(undefined, null), and assert get().userId is null while conversationRef remains unchanged.

minimum fix scope:
Update createTranscriptSessionState.update and add focused session state tests for undefined versus null.

## medium: Failed browser launch leaves OAuth callback promise alive

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-69aa448df6-0b50_a6d2ce7855
category: bug
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/main#2 (feat_library_69aa448df6)

evidence:
- frontend/src/main/openai_codex_oauth.cjs:163-165 (waitForOAuthCallback)
- frontend/src/main/openai_codex_oauth.cjs:268-287 (loginOpenAICodexOAuth)

loginOpenAICodexOAuth starts waitForOAuthCallback before launching the browser. If openExternal rejects, the catch closes the server and throws, but it never settles or catches waitForCallbackPromise. The promise remains pending until the 10 minute timeout, keeping the timeout handle alive and eventually rejecting without an await/catch path. That can produce an unhandled rejection and unnecessarily keep the process alive after a failed login launch.

recommendation:
Add a cancellation/finish path for the callback waiter when browser launch fails, or attach a catch to the waiter before throwing. The cleanup should clear the timeout and close the server through the same finish path used by normal completion.

test analysis:
No tests are included for openai_codex_oauth.cjs, and the listed scripts only expose Jest plus a shell smoke test.

suggested regression test:
Add a Jest test that injects a rejecting openExternal and fake timers, asserts loginOpenAICodexOAuth rejects with the launch error, and asserts no later unhandled callback timeout rejection is produced.

minimum fix scope:
frontend/src/main/openai_codex_oauth.cjs plus focused Jest coverage.

repro:
Use a test openExternal implementation that rejects. The function throws immediately, while the callback wait promise remains scheduled until OPENAI_CODEX_LOGIN_TIMEOUT_MS and then rejects independently.

## medium: Fallback OCR can cache results under the wrong screenshot

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-5186d21051-072c_e409103ed0
category: bug
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/agent/tools/preparation/ocr (feat_library_5186d21051)

evidence:
- backend/src/agent/tools/preparation/ocr/coordinator.py:79-90 (OcrCoordinator.get_ocr_results)
- backend/src/agent/tools/preparation/ocr/coordinator.py:112-119 (OcrCoordinator.get_ocr_results)

When a caller requests OCR for a screenshot_id that no longer matches the runtime state's current screenshot, the coordinator correctly avoids using cached results. It then performs OCR on the caller-provided screenshot_data, but stores those results with ocr_state.set_results() while the runtime current_screenshot_id still points at a different screenshot. That can poison the current screenshot cache with OCR extracted from the requested, now-stale screenshot, causing a later call for the actual current screenshot to reuse mismatched OCR results.

recommendation:
Do not write fallback OCR results into the shared current-screenshot cache when the requested screenshot_id differs from the OCR state's current screenshot. Either store results keyed by the requested screenshot_id, update the state with the matching screenshot id atomically, or return the fallback results without caching in the mismatch path.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for this feature, and the excerpt shows no regression coverage for screenshot-id mismatch followed by fallback OCR and subsequent cache reuse.

suggested regression test:
Add a unit test where the requested screenshot_id differs from ocr_state.get_current_screenshot_id(), fallback OCR returns a sentinel result for the requested screenshot, and the test asserts that the current screenshot cache is not populated with that sentinel result.

minimum fix scope:
Limit the fix to OcrCoordinator.get_ocr_results cache writes around the screenshot mismatch fallback path, plus a focused coordinator test with a fake session and OCR runtime state.

repro:
Call get_ocr_results(session, screenshot_data_for_A, screenshot_id='A') after ocr_state.get_current_screenshot_id() has advanced to 'B'. The fallback performs OCR on A's image and then stores those results via set_results() while the OCR state is still current for B. A later call for B can read A's OCR from get_results().

## medium: Fallback tool-call IDs are written to history but never staged for the matching tool output

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-a1389021e2-6a24_66618b2794
category: bug
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/agent/execution (feat_library_a1389021e2)

evidence:
- backend/src/agent/execution/tool_call_bridge.py:144-146 (to_history_tool_calls)
- backend/src/agent/execution/tool_call_bridge.py:178-187 (extract_tool_call_ids)
- backend/src/agent/execution/interaction_loop.py:461-468 (_commit_assistant_tool_turn)
- tests/backend/test_interaction_loop.py:383-390 (test_interaction_loop_replays_invalid_direct_mouse_tool_call)

When a parsed tool call has no metadata tool_call_id, to_history_tool_calls persists a fallback ID such as tool_call_0, but extract_tool_call_ids only returns IDs already present in metadata. _commit_assistant_tool_turn therefore records assistant tool_calls with fallback IDs while staging an empty ID list. Tool outputs added afterward cannot be linked to the assistant tool call, which can corrupt the LLM conversation history format for providers that require each tool output to reference a previous tool_call id.

recommendation:
Make the ID derivation shared between to_history_tool_calls and extract_tool_call_ids, or have _commit_assistant_tool_turn build history tool calls once and stage the exact same IDs it persists. Preserve provider IDs when present and use deterministic fallback IDs when absent.

test analysis:
The current tests only assert staging for native tool calls that include explicit ids, e.g. call_mouse_1 and call_browser_invalid_1. They do not cover a tool call without an id, where the history fallback and staged IDs diverge.

suggested regression test:
Add an InteractionLoop test whose LLM payload returns a tool call without an id, then assert the assistant history stores id tool_call_0 and stage_tool_call_ids receives ["tool_call_0"].

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/agent/execution/tool_call_bridge.py plus a focused test in tests/backend/test_interaction_loop.py

## medium: Finalizer cannot close local provider HTTP clients

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-b90075c5e0-2e10_b567c5a504
category: performance
confidence: high
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/llm/providers#1 (feat_library_b90075c5e0)

evidence:
- backend/src/llm/providers/local.py:33-38 (LocalLLMProvider.__init__)
- backend/src/llm/providers/local.py:67-86 (LocalLLMProvider._cleanup_http_client_finalizer)
- backend/src/llm/providers/__init__.py:107-115 (_create_cached_provider_factory)

The local provider registers a weakref finalizer whose only way to reach the provider is weakref.ref(self). When weakref.finalize invokes the callback, the provider object is already being finalized, so provider_weakref() can be None and the callback returns before closing the captured httpx.AsyncClient. Because provider instances are cached and can later be evicted by the bounded lru_cache, evicted local providers can leave connection pools and file descriptors open despite the comment claiming the finalizer prevents this leak.

recommendation:
Do not depend on recovering the provider from a weakref during finalization. Capture only the client/loop cleanup state needed by the finalizer, or provide an explicit provider-factory cache close/clear path that awaits _close_http_client before eviction/shutdown. If the client is lazily created, update the finalizer registration when the client is created or use a small cleanup holder object that owns the client reference.

test analysis:
The included tests only cover provider-native thinking request parameters and do not instantiate LocalLLMProvider, create an HTTP client, evict cached providers, or assert client closure.

suggested regression test:
Add a backend test that creates a local provider, forces _get_http_client(), evicts or deletes the provider, runs garbage collection/event-loop cleanup, and asserts the httpx client is closed or that an explicit cache shutdown path closes it.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/llm/providers/local.py and, if adding explicit factory cache cleanup, backend/src/llm/providers/__init__.py

## medium: Gate state is computed from stale permission statuses

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-f2d972a808-6b18_e41de0bbc9
category: bug
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/features/permissions (feat_library_f2d972a808)

evidence:
- frontend/src/renderer/features/permissions/stores/permissionStore.js:66-84 (buildStatusStateUpdate)
- frontend/src/renderer/features/permissions/stores/permissionStore.js:162-184 (runPermissionProbe/requestPermission)
- frontend/src/renderer/features/permissions/stores/permissionStore.js:192-203 (recheckAllPermissions)

buildStatusStateUpdate merges or replaces incoming statuses into a local statusesByPermissionId object, but resolveGateState is called with currentState.statusesByPermissionId instead of that newly computed object. Any probe, request, or recheck updates the displayed status map while leaving requiredPermissionIds, missingRequiredPermissions, needsOnboarding, and completedForManifest derived from the old statuses. This can keep onboarding/runtime gating stale after a permission is granted or revoked until a later full bootstrap or unrelated recalculation.

recommendation:
Pass the newly computed statusesByPermissionId into resolveGateState inside buildStatusStateUpdate. Keep the update helper as the single place where status map and gate state are derived together.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for the permission store helper or for gate-state updates after runPermissionProbe, requestPermission, or recheckAllPermissions. A test that asserted both the status map and missingRequiredPermissions after a returned grant would catch this.

suggested regression test:
Add a store/unit test that starts with one required permission missing, returns a granted status from RUN_PERMISSION_PROBE or CHECK_PERMISSIONS, and asserts statusesByPermissionId[id].granted is true and missingRequiredPermissions no longer contains that id.

minimum fix scope:
One-line fix in buildStatusStateUpdate plus a focused permissionStore regression test.

repro:
Grant a required permission through requestPermission or re-run all checks so the returned status has granted=true. The status badge updates from the new status map, but missingRequiredPermissions is recalculated against the previous map and can still list the permission as missing.

evidence:
- backend/src/services/ocr/ocr_service.py:287-300 (OcrService._build_ocr_results)
- backend/src/services/ocr/ocr_service.py:362-368 (OcrService._build_bbox_list)
- tests/backend/test_ocr_service.py:199-219 (test_build_ocr_results_maps_valid_rows_and_skips_invalid_rows)

_build_bbox_list drops invalid boxes and returns a compacted list, then _build_ocr_results zips the original text_list with that compacted bbox_list. If an invalid polygon appears before later valid rows, the later valid bounding boxes are paired with earlier text values, corrupting OCR coordinates. The included test only covers an invalid trailing row, so it does not exercise the index-shift case.

recommendation:
Build records by iterating original OCR rows by index and parsing each row's box in place. Skip only the row whose text/box/score is invalid so remaining rows preserve their original alignment.

test analysis:
The existing regression has a valid first row and invalid second row only, so compaction does not cause a later valid box to be assigned to the wrong text.

suggested regression test:
Add a test where an invalid box is in the middle of three OCR rows and assert the third text keeps the third box while the middle row is skipped.

minimum fix scope:
Change _build_ocr_results to parse boxes per original row index, or return index-preserving parsed boxes from _build_bbox_list.

repro:
Use txts=['first','bad','third'] and boxes=[valid_first, invalid_box, valid_third]. _build_bbox_list returns [valid_first, valid_third], then zip pairs 'bad' with valid_third and omits 'third'.

## medium: IPC initialization cannot be retried after initializer failure

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-1346364f94-7d38_f16e5b79db
category: bug
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/main/:surface (feat_library_1346364f94)

evidence:
- frontend/src/main/surface_runtime.cjs:473-479 (initializeMainProcessIpcOnce)

initializeMainProcessIpcOnce flips mainProcessIpcHandlersInitialized to true before calling initializer. If initializer throws partway through registration, the runtime is left marked initialized and every later call returns false, preventing recovery or a clean retry of missing handlers in the same process.

recommendation:
Set the initialized flag only after initializer completes successfully, or catch errors, reset the flag, and rethrow so callers can retry or fail clearly.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for this feature, and there is no evidence of a test covering a throwing initializer path.

suggested regression test:
Add a unit test that passes an initializer which throws, verifies a second call attempts initialization again, then verifies a successful initializer prevents further retries.

minimum fix scope:
Change initializeMainProcessIpcOnce error handling and add a focused test for thrown and successful initializer cases.

## medium: JSON schema conditional rules do not enforce required fields when defaulted grounding modes are omitted

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-a2349defde-4b06_c295f84a20
category: api-contract
confidence: high
triage: contract-mismatch
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/tools/computer (feat_library_a2349defde)

evidence:
- backend/src/tools/computer/grounding_contract.py:163-168 (build_source_grounding_json_properties)
- backend/src/tools/computer/grounding_contract.py:202-230 (build_source_grounding_json_rules)
- backend/src/tools/computer/grounding_contract.py:251-255 (build_drag_destination_json_properties)
- backend/src/tools/computer/grounding_contract.py:276-313 (build_drag_destination_json_rules)
- backend/src/tools/computer/schemas.py:57-64 (MouseControlArgs.validate_conditional_fields)
- backend/src/tools/computer/grounding_contract.py:116-120 (validate_source_grounding_fields)
- backend/src/tools/computer/grounding_contract.py:140-144 (validate_drag_destination_grounding_fields)

The exported JSON schema advertises default manual grounding, but the conditional rules only apply when find_coordinates_by or drag_to_find_coordinates_by is explicitly present. JSON Schema validators generally do not materialize defaults before evaluating if/then rules, so a payload can omit find_coordinates_by and x/y, or omit drag_to_find_coordinates_by and drag_to_x/drag_to_y for a drag action, while still passing the published schema. The Pydantic runtime defaults those modes to manual and then rejects the same payload. That creates a contract mismatch between model-facing tool schemas and backend runtime validation, causing avoidable tool-call failures for payloads that conform to the published schema.

recommendation:
Make the JSON schema rules match runtime defaults. For source grounding, require x/y whenever find_coordinates_by is absent or manual. For drag destination grounding, require drag_to_x/drag_to_y when action is drag and drag_to_find_coordinates_by is absent or manual. Alternatively, make the mode fields required in the JSON schema instead of relying on defaults.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for these schema builders or for parity between generated JSON schema validation and Pydantic validation.

suggested regression test:
Add a schema-parity test that validates example payloads against the generated JSON schema and the corresponding Pydantic models, including omitted find_coordinates_by and omitted drag_to_find_coordinates_by on drag actions. Assert both layers reject missing manual coordinates consistently.

minimum fix scope:
Update the JSON schema conditional rules in backend/src/tools/computer/grounding_contract.py and add focused parity coverage for MouseControlArgs and any tool schema that consumes these helper rules.

## medium: JSON suppression mishandles the opening brace and drops or suppresses following text

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-6a38404de9-8510_bca84a25c5
category: bug
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/api/processing/tts (feat_library_6a38404de9)

evidence:
- backend/src/api/processing/tts/processor.py:148-176
- backend/src/api/processing/tts/processor.py:207-217
- backend/src/api/processing/tts/processor.py:259-289

The processor sets _json_brace_depth to 1 when it sees an opening brace, but in the mid-chunk path it recursively processes post_marker including that same opening brace. The JSON suppression branch increments depth again, so a normal one-object JSON payload never returns to zero on its closing brace and all following text in that chunk is dropped. In the initial buffering path, a complete buffered JSON object with trailing natural language is dropped wholesale without scanning for the matching closing brace, so speech after JSON can be lost.

recommendation:
Use one consistent JSON parser state transition: either consume the opening brace before entering suppression with depth 1, or enter with depth 0 and let the suppression scanner count the included content. Also scan the current buffered content for JSON completion so trailing non-JSON text can be passed through.

test analysis:
No linked tests are present, and the edge cases with JSON beginning mid-chunk or completing in the initial buffer are not evidenced by any included test.

suggested regression test:
Add tests asserting that 'Intro {"x":1} outro' and '{"x":1} outro' suppress only the JSON object and still send the surrounding text to TTS.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/api/processing/tts/processor.py JSON brace-depth handling plus focused TTSProcessor tests.

repro:
After the processor is in text mode, process a chunk like 'Intro {"x":1} outro'. 'Intro ' is sent, but the opening brace is counted twice and ' outro' is not sent. Starting from the indeterminate state with '{"x":1} outro' drops the entire buffer and does not recover ' outro'.
