- frontend/src/renderer/types/backendEvents.ts:228-234 (isBackendEvent)

`isBackendEvent` is exported as a full `value is BackendEvent` guard, but it only verifies that `type` is one of the known strings. It does not verify that the event has an object-shaped payload, that payload fields have the expected primitive/container types, or that required top-level identity fields are well-formed when present. Callers that rely on this guard get TypeScript narrowing for the entire event union even when malformed backend or IPC data contains invalid payload shapes, which can turn a transport/schema mismatch into renderer-side runtime errors or incorrect UI state.

recommendation:
Either rename/scope the helper to a discriminator-only predicate, or add per-event payload validation for the fields the renderer relies on before returning `value is BackendEvent`. Keep the runtime contract aligned with the exported TypeScript type.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for this feature, and the shown package test scripts do not demonstrate coverage for malformed backend event payloads passing through this guard.

suggested regression test:
Add a frontend unit test for `isBackendEvent` that rejects known event types with invalid payload shapes, or, if intentionally discriminator-only, asserts the narrower helper name/return type so callers cannot treat it as full payload validation.

minimum fix scope:
Update `frontend/src/renderer/types/backendEvents.ts` and add focused frontend tests for valid and malformed backend event objects.

## medium: Backend-prioritized duplicate selection can discard model-visible tool output

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-ccb2a465dd-4355_02af08ca5f
category: data-loss
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source packages/windie-sdk-js/src/projections (feat_library_ccb2a465dd)

- packages/windie-sdk-js/src/projections/conversationProjections.ts:441-457 (toRehydrateMessages/buildRehydrateSnapshot)

Duplicate tool outputs are resolved by preferring backend-sourced events before checking whether either event has model-visible content. If a backend echo and a client/sidecar output share the same dedupe key, and only the non-backend event carries modelContent, the backend event wins anyway. buildRehydrateSnapshot then serializes the chosen tool_output with modelTextFromPayload, so the replay/rehydration message can contain an empty or less useful tool result even though a duplicate event with model-visible content was available.

recommendation:
Change duplicate selection so model-visible content is the primary criterion for rehydration, or split display and rehydrate dedupe policies. At minimum, do not allow backend source preference to override an existing output that has model content when the candidate lacks it.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for this feature, and the excerpt shows no assertion covering duplicate tool outputs where source priority conflicts with modelContent availability.

suggested regression test:
Add an SDK projection test with duplicate tool_output events sharing one dedupe key: a backend event without modelContent and a non-backend event with modelContent. Assert buildRehydrateSnapshot keeps the model-visible output content.

minimum fix scope:
packages/windie-sdk-js/src/projections/conversationProjections.ts

## medium: Best-effort runtime state seeding can still abort when reading existing state fails

## medium: Blind cast lets IPC registry drift reach runtime

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-092f3e0e96-64d3_003244c90f
category: api-contract
confidence: medium
triage: contract-mismatch
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/ipc (feat_library_092f3e0e96)

evidence:
- frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/ipc/channels.ts:6-86
- frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/ipc/bridge.ts:83-86

The renderer imports the shared IPC JSON and asserts it is a complete SharedChannelRegistry, so TypeScript will trust every listed channel key even if the JSON is missing, renamed, or has an unexpected value. A drifted entry would still compile, but consumers of INVOKE_CHANNELS/SEND_CHANNELS could receive undefined or a wrong channel string at runtime and then pass it into IpcBridge.invoke/send. In production, bridge validation is skipped, so the bad channel would be forwarded to window.ipc/preload instead of failing at module load with a clear parity error.

recommendation:
Replace the unchecked cast with a small runtime shape check for the expected SEND_CHANNELS, INVOKE_CHANNELS, and ON_CHANNELS keys, or generate the TypeScript registry/types from the shared JSON so missing keys fail before release. Keep the validation local to this boundary so renderer callers cannot observe a partially invalid registry.

test analysis:
The feature metadata includes no linked tests, and the package only exposes general Jest/typecheck scripts; the shown source has no regression test asserting the imported JSON contains every renderer-declared channel key.

suggested regression test:
Add a Jest test for frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/ipc/channels.ts that imports the channel module and asserts each expected registry key is present with the exact string value, plus a negative/unit test for the runtime validator if one is added.

minimum fix scope:
frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/ipc/channels.ts plus a focused frontend Jest test.

## medium: Boolean values are accepted for numeric-only settings

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-343861e455-d5fb_52c83037e4
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/core/validation (feat_library_343861e455)

evidence:
- backend/src/core/validation/settings_update_rules.py:18-19 (SETTINGS_UPDATE_RULES)
- backend/src/core/validation/settings_update_rules.py:50-56 (SETTINGS_UPDATE_RULES)
- backend/src/core/validation/validators.py:160-164 (validate_field)

Numeric settings are validated with isinstance(value, int) or isinstance(value, (int, float)). In Python, bool is a subclass of int, so True and False pass validation for timeout and token-count settings even though they are semantically boolean values, not numeric limits. That can persist invalid configuration such as history_compaction_trigger_tokens=True or llm_timeout=False.

recommendation:
For numeric settings, reject bool explicitly before the isinstance check or add a numeric validator/rule flag that treats bool as invalid for int and float fields.

test analysis:
The included tests verify allowed None, unknown-key behavior, and a valid boolean field, but they do not cover booleans supplied to numeric settings.

suggested regression test:
Add tests asserting that bool values are rejected for representative int-only and int-or-float settings while real int/float values remain accepted.

minimum fix scope:
Tighten numeric validation in validate_field or settings_update_rules and add focused tests for bool-as-int rejection.

repro:
Call validate_settings_update({"history_compaction_trigger_tokens": True}) or validate_settings_update({"llm_timeout": False}); the value passes the isinstance check for int.

## medium: Bootstrap status read can overwrite a newer backend status event

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-15a846a04c-7e37_7f43c430b8
category: concurrency
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/runtime (feat_library_15a846a04c)

evidence:
- frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/runtime/localBackendStatusStore.js:51-53 (ensureIpcSubscription)
- frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/runtime/localBackendStatusStore.js:56-72 (ensureBootstrapStatusRead)
- frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/runtime/localBackendStatusStore.js:85-89 (subscribeLocalBackendStatusStore)

subscribeLocalBackendStatusStore installs the live IPC listener and then starts an async bootstrap read. Because both paths directly call applySnapshot with no generation, timestamp, or freshness check, a stale GET_LOCAL_BACKEND_STATUS response can arrive after a newer LOCAL_BACKEND_STATUS event and overwrite it. That can make subscribers see ready=false/stopped even after the backend has already emitted ready=true.

recommendation:
Prevent bootstrap responses from overwriting newer events. Track a local revision that increments on IPC events and only apply the bootstrap result if no event has arrived since the bootstrap request started, or perform the bootstrap read before registering live events with an explicit ordering guarantee.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for this feature, so there is no first-class evidence that bootstrap/event ordering is exercised.

suggested regression test:
Add a Jest test for subscribeLocalBackendStatusStore that controls the invoke promise and listener callback, emits ready=true before resolving the bootstrap with ready=false, and asserts the final snapshot remains ready=true.

minimum fix scope:
frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/runtime/localBackendStatusStore.js plus a focused renderer store test.

repro:
Subscribe to the store. Let GET_LOCAL_BACKEND_STATUS remain pending, emit LOCAL_BACKEND_STATUS with ready=true, then resolve the bootstrap invoke with an older ready=false payload. The final snapshot becomes the stale bootstrap value.

## medium: Bulk stop is authorized only by a global API key while workspace scope comes from the request body

If that key is shared by workers, dashboards, or automation across workspaces, any valid caller can stop runs in another workspace by changing the body. This is a concrete trust-boundary risk because the authorization context is not bound to the workspace being stopped in the route.

## medium: Bundle preparation does not register resolved calls in session storage

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-02466c111f-db66_34065f66ab
category: api-contract
confidence: medium
triage: contract-mismatch
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/agent/tools/preparation (feat_library_02466c111f)

- backend/src/agent/tools/preparation/preparer.py:220-230 (ToolPreparer._prepare_bundle)
- backend/src/agent/tools/preparation/preparer.py:303-316 (ToolPreparer._prepare_single)

Single-tool preparation registers the resolved call in session runtime storage before returning, and the helper documents that as session runtime registration. Bundle preparation returns resolved calls but never invokes the same registration path for any bundled call. That creates inconsistent behavior between single and bundled calls: downstream code that relies on session.register_resolved_tool_call for lookup, replay, or request correlation will work for single calls and miss bundled calls.

recommendation:
Register each successfully resolved bundled call with the session using a stable execution identifier, or document and enforce that bundled calls must never be looked up through session runtime storage. Prefer using the existing registration helper if the contract is shared.

test analysis:
No linked tests were provided, and the excerpt shows no bundle test asserting session registration parity with single-call preparation.

suggested regression test:
Add a bundle preparation test with a fake session that records register_resolved_tool_call invocations and assert every resolved bundled call is registered with the expected bundle/request metadata.

minimum fix scope:
Update _prepare_bundle to register resolved calls consistently, plus a focused regression test for bundle registration behavior.

## medium: Canonical tool-output reuses client-supplied context after routing by request id

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-c349e3cc10-4f75_9818d9242e

The handler resolves the authoritative session using user_id and request_id, processes the result on that session, and then emits the canonical tool-output with session_id, conversation_ref, and turn_ref copied from the incoming frontend message. If the frontend sends stale or mismatched context fields for a valid request_id, the backend can apply the result to one session while publishing the canonical event under a different conversation or turn context. That can attach tool output to the wrong UI stream/history projection and makes request_id routing inconsistent with the emitted event contract.

recommendation:
Build the canonical event context from the resolved session/request metadata, or validate that the client-supplied session_id, conversation_ref, and turn_ref match the resolved request before emitting. Reject or sanitize mismatches.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for mismatched tool-result context fields or canonical tool-output routing.

suggested regression test:
Add a test where a valid request_id is submitted with mismatched conversation_ref/turn_ref and assert the canonical tool-output uses authoritative session metadata or the message is rejected.

minimum fix scope:
ToolResultHandler canonical event construction and, if necessary, AgentSession request metadata accessors.

repro:
Send a tool-result with a request_id that maps to an active session, but include a stale conversation_ref or turn_ref in the message. The session processing follows request_id, while the emitted tool-output includes the stale message fields.

## medium: Close can hang forever if the sender task is cancelled while close is waiting

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-c456e2729d-b87b_859dabd2e7
category: concurrency
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/api/transport (feat_library_c456e2729d)

evidence:
- backend/src/api/transport/websocket.py:166-214 (SafeWebSocket._sender_loop)
- backend/src/api/transport/websocket.py:284-319 (SafeWebSocket.close)

close() waits unconditionally on _close_event after trying to enqueue or directly close. _close_event is only set by the sender loop's finally block or the direct-close path when no sender task exists. However, _sender_loop catches ordinary send failures but does not catch asyncio.CancelledError, which is a BaseException in modern Python. If the background sender task is externally cancelled while close() is in progress, the finally block should normally run, but if the task was never allowed to reach or complete that finally before close waits, or if cancellation happens around task creation/loop scheduling, close has no timeout or task-await fallback and can block the caller indefinitely. This is most visible during shutdown paths where task cancellation and graceful close often race.

recommendation:
Make close robust against sender-task cancellation by awaiting the sender task with cancellation handling or adding a bounded wait around _close_event.wait(), and ensure close always completes after attempting direct close. Also consider explicitly handling asyncio.CancelledError in the sender loop to store a terminal sender error before setting the close event.

test analysis:
No linked tests are provided for cancellation or shutdown races around SafeWebSocket.close().

suggested regression test:
Create a SafeWebSocket, start a send so the sender task exists, cancel _sender_task, then assert close() returns within a short timeout and does not hang.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/api/transport/websocket.py close and sender-loop shutdown handling

## medium: Concurrent screenshot tool preparation can double-hide the surface before any token is registered

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-5eddbd52b0-d0e8_0156193708
category: concurrency
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/services/surfaceOrchestrator#1 (feat_library_5eddbd52b0)

- frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/services/surfaceOrchestrator/toolLifecycle.ts:75-91 (prepareToolExecutionSurface)
- frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/services/surfaceOrchestrator/toolLifecycle.ts:101 (prepareToolExecutionSurface)
- frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/services/surfaceOrchestrator/state.ts:37-57 (registerSurfaceToken/releaseSurfaceToken)

The decision to collapse for a screenshot is based on !hasActiveSurfaceTokens(), but the token is not registered until after the async dashboard handoff and async suppressSurfaceForBackgroundCapture calls complete. Two concurrent screenshot preparations can both observe no active tokens, both enter the handoff/collapse path, and only then register tokens. That makes the active-token guard ineffective during the critical async window and can produce duplicate hide operations or overwrite the single pendingHiddenSurfaceRestore value, leaving restore behavior dependent on whichever preparation finishes last.

recommendation:
Reserve a surface token, or another explicit in-flight preparation marker, before the first await in the mode !== 'none' path. Use that reservation to make the collapse/handoff decision atomic for overlapping preparations, and release it on preparation failure.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for overlapping prepareToolExecutionSurface calls, and the package scripts only show generic Jest entrypoints rather than coverage for this orchestrator race.

suggested regression test:
Add a Jest test that stubs suppressSurfaceForBackgroundCapture as a deferred promise, starts two screenshot prepareToolExecutionSurface calls concurrently, and asserts the suppress/handoff path runs only once while both preparations receive valid tokens and final restore occurs once.

minimum fix scope:
frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/services/surfaceOrchestrator/toolLifecycle.ts and a focused frontend test for concurrent screenshot preparation.

## medium: Cross-window config sync can re-broadcast unchanged nested provider config indefinitely

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-9a49786b23-133a_dbca6e593b
category: concurrency
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/app/providers (feat_library_9a49786b23)

evidence:
- frontend/src/renderer/app/providers/AppConfigProvider.jsx:102-107 (commitFrontendConfig)
- frontend/src/renderer/app/providers/AppConfigProvider.jsx:248-267 (storage event handler)
- frontend/src/renderer/app/providers/appConfigPersistence.js:107-123 (mergeFrontendProviderConfig)
- frontend/src/renderer/app/providers/configComparison.ts:15-19 (hasShallowConfigChanges)

The storage-event path receives a localStorage update from another renderer, rebuilds the merged config, then calls applyResolvedConfig with persistToDisk false and syncBackend false. However commitFrontendConfig still always writes the config back to localStorage before checking persistToDisk, so a passive storage sync re-broadcasts the same config. For configs containing nested provider_api_keys or provider_oauth, mergeFrontendProviderConfig creates fresh nested objects even when their contents are unchanged, while hasShallowConfigChanges compares those object values by reference. That makes an unchanged storage event look like a real config change, so two windows can repeatedly trigger each other with localStorage writes.

recommendation:
Do not write back to localStorage when applying a storage-event snapshot, or add an explicit persistToLocalStorage option and set it false for storage sync. Also use content-aware comparison for nested provider config so unchanged provider_api_keys/provider_oauth snapshots are not treated as changes only because object references differ.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for multi-window storage synchronization or unchanged nested provider config comparisons.

suggested regression test:
Add a provider-level unit test that simulates a storage event with unchanged provider_api_keys/provider_oauth and asserts saveConfigToStorage is not called again; add a companion test proving equivalent nested provider config does not count as changed.

minimum fix scope:
AppConfigProvider commit/apply options plus config comparison or provider merge behavior for nested provider config.

repro:
Open two renderer windows with a config containing provider_api_keys or provider_oauth. Change a setting in one window. The other window handles the storage event, creates fresh nested provider config objects, treats them as changed, writes localStorage again, and can trigger the first window to repeat the same cycle.

## medium: Declared tool permissions are treated as authorization grants

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-0542387a45-c197_c99241e7eb
category: security
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/core/security (feat_library_0542387a45)

evidence:
- backend/src/core/security/policy.py:198-228 (SecurityPolicy.check_permission)

check_permission derives the required permission set from tool metadata or a local dictionary, then grants access whenever the requested permission is present in that set. That verifies a tool declared the capability, but it does not verify that the user, session, role, policy, or deployment actually authorized that capability. A tool that is registered with broad required_permissions is therefore allowed to perform those actions unconditionally unless separately blocked by name.

recommendation:
Separate capability declaration from authorization. Keep required_permissions as the tool's requested capability set, but compare it against an explicit allow policy for the current user/session/context or a trusted registry-managed grant set before returning true.

test analysis:
No linked permission-policy tests are included, and the implementation comment states that user/role-based permission checking is not implemented.

suggested regression test:
Add a test with a tool declaring NETWORK_ACCESS or EXECUTE_COMMANDS where the active policy has not granted that permission, and assert check_permission returns false until an explicit grant is configured.

minimum fix scope:
SecurityPolicy permission model and tests for denied-by-default authorization grants.

## medium: Default session creation bypasses the DI LLM client provider

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-467ec5c053-b1d2_105ccb1cd3
category: api-contract
confidence: medium
triage: contract-mismatch
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/core/container/:session (feat_library_467ec5c053)

evidence:
- backend/src/core/container/session_factory.py:80-87
- backend/src/core/container/session_runtime.py:66-78

AgentSessionFactory always resolves a non-null session_config, even when the caller did not pass a session-specific override, then calls llm_client_factory(session_config). That means SessionRuntimeCoordinator.llm_client_factory always takes the session_config branch and calls either _mock_llm_factory or get_llm_client(session_config), while the DI-container path at line 78 is unreachable for normal default sessions. This contradicts the local comment that the DI container factory may be overridden for simulation and can break dependency injection, test overrides, or runtime-provided LLM clients for default sessions.

recommendation:
Preserve the distinction between no override and explicit session config. For default sessions, call the LLM factory without an argument so the DI container provider is used; only pass config when create_agent_session receives a real session-specific override. Alternatively make the coordinator factory explicitly route the factory's base config through the same DI override path.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for this feature, and the unreachable DI branch would require a test that creates a default session with an overridden DI LLM provider and asserts that provider is used.

suggested regression test:
Add a focused session runtime test with a fake container whose _di_container.llm_client returns a sentinel client, call create_agent_session without a config override, and assert the session receives the sentinel instead of a get_llm_client-created client. Add a second case proving an explicit config override still uses the config-aware path.

minimum fix scope:
Update AgentSessionFactory.create_session and/or SessionRuntimeCoordinator.llm_client_factory so default config use and explicit config override remain distinguishable, then add focused tests for both paths.

## medium: Deferred ElevenLabs audio stream can hang forever when no text is produced

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-4cad86d91f-f71e_5a7a51b30d
category: concurrency
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/api/services (feat_library_4cad86d91f)

evidence:
- backend/src/api/services/tts_session.py:22-33 (_DeferredSpeechService._ensure_service)
- backend/src/api/services/tts_session.py:56-62 (_DeferredSpeechService.stream_audio)
- backend/src/api/services/tts_session.py:82-94 (TTSSession.__aenter__)
- tests/backend/test_tts_session.py:58-100 (test_tts_session_defers_elevenlabs_initialize_until_first_text_chunk)

For ElevenLabs, TTSSession starts the audio streaming task immediately with a deferred proxy, but the proxy's stream_audio waits on _activation_event until process_text initializes the real service. If a query or wakeword path creates the session but never sends text to TTS, stream_audio has no completion path other than task cancellation during cleanup. That leaves a per-request task blocked for the entire request lifetime and makes any manager path that expects the stream generator to finish naturally vulnerable to hangs. The existing test asserts lazy initialization on first text but does not exercise the no-text path or verify stream_audio can terminate without activation.

recommendation:
Give the deferred proxy an explicit closed/no-activation path, such as setting a shutdown event in shutdown/cleanup and having stream_audio return when either activation or shutdown occurs. Alternatively, do not start the streaming task for the deferred ElevenLabs proxy until the first text chunk initializes a real service.

test analysis:
The TTS tests cover deferred initialization after process_text and cleanup cancellation, but they never iterate stream_audio when no text arrives, so the permanently waiting activation_event path is untested.

suggested regression test:
Add an async test that enters an ElevenLabs TTSSession, never calls process_text, invokes/starts stream_audio, exits the session, and asserts the stream task completes or is cleanly cancelled without waiting indefinitely.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/api/services/tts_session.py plus tests/backend/test_tts_session.py

## medium: Drag-destination-only tools are forced through source-coordinate validation

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-b826096e8e-52fd_d1f9a44942
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/agent/tools/preparation/helpers (feat_library_b826096e8e)

evidence:
- backend/src/agent/tools/preparation/helpers/preparation_helper.py:71-94 (resolve_tool_with_coordinates)
- backend/src/agent/tools/preparation/helpers/grounded_source_preparation.py:207-227 (resolve_grounded_source_coordinates)
- backend/src/agent/tools/preparation/helpers/mouse_drag_destination_preparation.py:102-128 (resolve_mouse_drag_destination_coordinates)

The facade always calls source-coordinate preparation before drag-destination preparation. The source helper does not first check whether the tool actually supports source grounding; non-OCR/PREDICTION calls fall into the manual branch and require top-level x/y. A tool that only needs drag destination grounding, or a drag call whose source is already executable but whose destination needs resolution, can fail with 'Manual coordinates require numeric x and y values' before the destination helper gets a chance to resolve drag_to_* coordinates. The separate supports_drag_destination_grounding gate in the destination helper indicates destination grounding is an independent capability, but the call order makes it dependent on source-coordinate validity.

recommendation:
Gate resolve_grounded_source_coordinates behind supports_source_grounding or tool_call_has_manual/source-resolution need, then run drag-destination resolution independently when supports_drag_destination_grounding applies.

test analysis:
The included coordinate-resolution tests cover OCR/PREDICTION source paths only and do not exercise resolve_tool_with_coordinates with a drag-destination grounding case that lacks valid top-level x/y.

suggested regression test:
Add a backend test for resolve_tool_with_coordinates where a drag action has drag_to_find_coordinates_by requiring OCR/PREDICTION and no top-level x/y; assert destination coordinates are resolved without the source helper raising.

minimum fix scope:
preparation_helper.py and/or grounded_source_preparation.py, plus a focused backend regression test.

## medium: Duplicate future creation can orphan the first waiter

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-0d5e259db8-4dfa_5b0cfe6623
category: concurrency
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/agent/tools/waiting (feat_library_0d5e259db8)

evidence:
- backend/src/agent/tools/waiting/storage/result_storage.py:111-125 (ToolResultStorage.create_result_future)
- backend/src/agent/tools/waiting/storage/result_storage.py:219-233 (ToolResultStorage.create_bundle_future)
- backend/src/agent/tools/waiting/storage/result_storage.py:127-146 (ToolResultStorage.resolve_result_future)
- backend/src/agent/tools/waiting/storage/result_storage.py:235-254 (ToolResultStorage.resolve_bundle_future)

create_result_future and create_bundle_future unconditionally overwrite the dict entry for a correlation id. If two waiters are registered for the same request or bundle id, the first Future remains pending but is no longer reachable from storage, so resolve_result_future/resolve_bundle_future can only complete the most recently created Future. This can hang the earlier caller and leak the orphaned Future.

recommendation:
Make duplicate future registration explicit: either return the existing pending Future for the same id, reject duplicates with an exception, or complete/cancel the replaced Future before overwriting it. Mirror the chosen behavior for bundle futures.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for duplicate request_id or bundle_id waiter registration.

suggested regression test:
Add tests that call create_result_future and create_bundle_future twice with the same id and assert the first waiter is not left pending under the chosen contract.

minimum fix scope:
Update duplicate handling in create_result_future and create_bundle_future, plus focused storage tests.

repro:
Call create_result_future('r1') twice, keep both returned futures, then call resolve_result_future('r1', result). The second future resolves and the first remains pending indefinitely.

## medium: ElevenLabs speech backend becomes permanently silent after the first flush

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-f841033d5b-bb69_aa5a727cc6
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/core/services (feat_library_f841033d5b)

evidence:
- backend/src/core/services/elevenlabs_tts_service.py:92-108 (ElevenLabsTTSService.flush)
- backend/src/core/services/elevenlabs_tts_service.py:184-197 (ElevenLabsTTSService._send_text_chunk)
- backend/src/core/services/elevenlabs_tts_service.py:199-225 (ElevenLabsTTSService._receive_audio_loop)
- tests/backend/test_elevenlabs_tts_service.py:55-104 (test_elevenlabs_tts_service_streams_incremental_text_chunks_and_flush)

flush() marks _eos_sent True and sends the terminal empty text frame, while the receive loop exits on isFinal, sets running False, enqueues the stream sentinel, and closes the websocket. _send_text_chunk then silently returns forever when _eos_sent is true. Unlike the local TTSService, where flush only drains the current buffered text and the worker can continue accepting later text, the ElevenLabs provider cannot speak a second assistant response unless the entire service is reinitialized. The included ElevenLabs test validates only the first utterance and flush path, so it does not contradict this but misses the reuse contract implied by process_text/flush as a runtime speech service.

recommendation:
Make the ElevenLabs provider support a new generation after flush, either by reopening a websocket session and resetting _eos_sent/running before accepting new text, or by documenting and enforcing one-shot lifecycle semantics at the SpeechService factory/caller boundary. Prefer provider parity with TTSService so flush finalizes only the current output, not the whole backend.

test analysis:
The current ElevenLabs tests assert one initialization, several process_text calls, one flush, and stream termination. They never call process_text after flush or assert that multiple assistant responses can be spoken by the same service instance.

suggested regression test:
Add an async ElevenLabs test that initializes the service, processes and flushes a first utterance, then processes a second utterance on the same instance and asserts a new websocket session or successful second text send occurs without silent return.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/core/services/elevenlabs_tts_service.py plus tests/backend/test_elevenlabs_tts_service.py

repro:
Initialize ElevenLabsTTSService, call process_text('first'), flush(), then call process_text('second') on the same service. The second call reaches _send_text_chunk with _eos_sent true and sends no websocket message; after the receive loop finalizes, running is also false.

## medium: Embedding space version can disagree with the returned vector dimension

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-bfae81359d-676f_534c6ca6cd
category: api-contract
confidence: medium
triage: contract-mismatch
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/api/routes/memory (feat_library_bfae81359d)

evidence:
- backend/src/api/routes/memory/embeddings/service.py:27-36 (resolve_embedding_space_version)
- backend/src/api/routes/memory/embeddings/service.py:56-75 (generate_embedding_response)
- backend/src/api/routes/memory/embeddings/service.py:82-98 (resolve_health_payload)

The response dimension is computed from the actual embedding length, but embedding_space_version is built from embedding_provider.dimension. When the provider does not expose dimension, exposes a stale dimension, or changes runtime fallback behavior, the same response can report dimension=len(embedding_list) while embedding_space_version contains unknown-dimension or a different provider dimension. Since the route documents this version as the compatibility key for local FAISS indexes, a mismatched version can make clients reuse incompatible indexes or unnecessarily rebuild compatible ones.

recommendation:
Derive embedding_space_version from the actual returned embedding dimension in response-producing paths, or verify provider.dimension matches len(embedding_list) and fail with a clear provider contract error when it does not.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for providers without a dimension attribute or with a dimension that differs from the returned embedding length.

suggested regression test:
Add service tests with a fake provider returning a 3-element embedding while dimension is missing or set to a conflicting value; assert embedding_space_version uses or validates the actual returned dimension consistently with response.dimension.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/api/routes/memory/embeddings/service.py and focused embedding service tests.

## medium: Event handler deduplication uses unstable ids for weak bound methods

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-2fdb111c39-48d6_e4a09d76dd
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/core/infrastructure (feat_library_2fdb111c39)

evidence:
- backend/src/core/infrastructure/event_bus_registry.py:30-44 (EventHandlerWrapper.handler)
- backend/src/core/infrastructure/event_bus_registry.py:167-180 (EventHandlerStore._dedupe_handlers)
- backend/src/core/infrastructure/event_bus_registry.py:114-126 (EventHandlerStore.resolve_handlers)

Bound method handlers are stored via WeakMethod, and each access to wrapper.handler can materialize a fresh bound-method object. _dedupe_handlers then records id(handler), which is only stable for that transient object, not for the underlying instance/function pair. During MRO handler resolution this can fail to deduplicate the same subscribed method consistently, and object-id reuse can also incorrectly collapse distinct bound handlers. The result is incorrect publish behavior: duplicate handler execution or skipped handlers when base and subclass subscriptions are resolved together.

recommendation:
Deduplicate handlers by a stable identity. For bound methods, use a tuple of the target instance identity and function identity, or keep a stable key on EventHandlerWrapper when it is constructed. For plain functions, the function object identity is sufficient.

test analysis:
The included tests do not cover EventBus or EventHandlerStore behavior, inheritance-based handler resolution, weak bound handlers, or duplicate subscription semantics.

suggested regression test:
Add EventHandlerStore/EventBus tests with bound methods subscribed on both a base event and derived event, asserting intended single execution, and with two different instances using the same method asserting neither is dropped.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/core/infrastructure/event_bus_registry.py plus event bus registry tests.

repro:
Subscribe bound instance methods across an event class hierarchy, then publish a subclass event so resolve_handlers aggregates multiple subscriber lists and _dedupe_handlers processes WeakMethod-backed wrappers.

## medium: Failed OpenAI transcription items leave partial transcripts retained

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-9a79195fcc-45f9_507e3e641e
category: performance
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/api/services/transcription (feat_library_9a79195fcc)

- backend/src/api/services/transcription/openai_realtime.py:141-148 (OpenAIRealtimeTranscriptionSession.stream_events)

Delta events append text into _partial_transcripts by item_id, and completed events explicitly pop that item. The failed-event branch emits an error but does not remove the failed item_id from _partial_transcripts, so repeated failed transcription items after partial deltas can retain transcript text for the lifetime of the websocket session.

recommendation:
In the failed-event branch, read item_id and pop it from _partial_transcripts before or after sending the error event, matching the completed branch cleanup behavior.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for OpenAI realtime event handling, and the excerpt shows no regression test asserting partial transcript cleanup on failed events.

suggested regression test:
Add a unit test for stream_events using a fake websocket that emits a delta followed by a failed event for the same item_id, then assert the session emits an error and no partial transcript entry remains.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/api/services/transcription/openai_realtime.py failed-event handling plus a focused backend unit test.

repro:
Drive stream_events with a delta event for item_id X followed by conversation.item.input_audio_transcription.failed for the same item_id, then inspect that _partial_transcripts still contains X.

## medium: Failed proactive OCR task bypasses the intended on-demand fallback

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-5186d21051-ef52_5d3dd600d2
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/agent/tools/preparation/ocr (feat_library_5186d21051)

evidence:
- backend/src/agent/tools/preparation/ocr/coordinator.py:56-75 (OcrCoordinator.get_ocr_results)
- backend/src/agent/tools/preparation/ocr/coordinator.py:96-112 (OcrCoordinator.get_ocr_results)

The coordinator waits for an in-flight proactive OCR task and only handles asyncio.TimeoutError. If that task fails with any other exception, the exception propagates immediately and the code never reaches the on-demand OCR fallback. This contradicts the fallback path's stated role for cases where proactive OCR is disabled, failed, or unavailable, and can turn a recoverable proactive-task failure into a user-visible failure even when ocr_service.perform_ocr(screenshot_data) would succeed.

recommendation:
Catch proactive-task failures separately from timeout, log them, and continue into the existing cache check and on-demand OCR path. Preserve cancellation semantics by not swallowing asyncio.CancelledError unless there is an explicit reason to do so.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included, and the excerpt has no coverage demonstrating behavior when the proactive OCR task raises before cached OCR results are available.

suggested regression test:
Add a coordinator test with an active OCR task that raises a non-cancellation exception and an enabled fake OCR service; assert that get_ocr_results returns the fallback service result and logs or records the proactive failure.

minimum fix scope:
Update the active_ocr_task wait block in OcrCoordinator.get_ocr_results and add one focused failure-path test.

repro:
Set ocr_state.get_active_task(screenshot_id) to return a task that raises RuntimeError, while the OCR service is enabled and perform_ocr would return valid results. get_ocr_results raises RuntimeError instead of running the fallback OCR path.

## medium: FileConversationStore can lose events during concurrent appends

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-bb833ffb1e-0ee3_acf9c096b4
category: data-loss
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node package @windie/sdk (feat_library_bb833ffb1e)

evidence:
- packages/windie-sdk-js/src/stores/FileConversationStore.ts:147-174 (FileConversationStore.appendEvents)
- packages/windie-sdk-js/src/stores/FileConversationStore.ts:297-319 (FileConversationStore.readConversation/writeConversation)

appendEvents performs a read-modify-write per conversation without any per-file serialization. Two concurrent appendEvent/appendEvents calls for the same conversation can both read the same stored event list, each merge its own unique event, then the last rename wins and drops the other event. The temporary-file rename makes each write atomic, but it does not prevent lost updates across overlapping reads and writes.

recommendation:
Serialize writes per conversationRef inside FileConversationStore, for example with a per-conversation promise queue around the read/merge/write section. Keep the atomic temp-file rename for crash safety.

test analysis:
The feature metadata lists no linked tests, and the implementation excerpt does not show any concurrent append coverage for the file-backed store.

suggested regression test:
Add a FileConversationStore test that fires many appendEvent calls for the same conversationRef concurrently, then asserts every unique event is present after loadEvents().

minimum fix scope:
Add per-conversation write serialization in FileConversationStore.appendEvents and a focused concurrent append regression test.

repro:
Call appendEvent(eventA) and appendEvent(eventB) concurrently on the same FileConversationStore and conversationRef while the file initially has the same contents for both calls; whichever write finishes last can overwrite the other event.

## medium: find-text-candidates echoes threshold but returns matches below it

id: fnd_sig-feat-route-347a74465f-e2e45e_9c6aa2adf6
category: api-contract
confidence: medium
triage: contract-mismatch
status: open

- backend/src/api/routes/sdk/router.py:182-195 (sdk_ocr_find_text_candidates)

The candidates route accepts the same OcrTextQueryRequest and returns the same OcrFindTextResponse shape as /ocr/find-text, including the echoed threshold, but it slices the ranked list without applying request.threshold. The sibling /ocr/find-text route filters matches by threshold before applying max_results. A caller that treats the shared response contract consistently can receive low-confidence matches while the response says a threshold was used, which is risky for OCR-driven automation that may resolve or click text based on these candidates. Confidence is medium because the route name may intend broader candidate discovery, but the shared request/response contract and echoed threshold make the current behavior ambiguous and easy to misuse.

recommendation:
Either apply the same threshold filter before slicing candidates, or make the API contract explicit by using a candidates-specific request/response field that does not imply threshold filtering. If unfiltered candidates are intentional, rename or document the threshold semantics in the route contract and avoid echoing it as if it constrained matches.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for this feature, and there is no evidence in the provided excerpt of a regression test asserting whether find-text-candidates should honor or ignore threshold.

suggested regression test:
Add a route-level test that stubs OCR results with one match above and one below a requested threshold, calls POST /api/sdk/ocr/find-text-candidates, and asserts the intended threshold behavior explicitly.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/api/routes/sdk/router.py plus a focused backend route test for POST /api/sdk/ocr/find-text-candidates.

## medium: Gemini streaming drops native web search option

category: bug

## medium: Handshake failure leaves socket open but session treated as connected

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-4ff8786511-8ed6_3954173e8f
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source packages/windie-sdk-js/src/transport (feat_library_4ff8786511)

evidence:
- packages/windie-sdk-js/src/transport/ManagedBackendSession.ts:131-146 (ManagedBackendSession.connect)
- packages/windie-sdk-js/src/transport/ManagedBackendSession.ts:231-249 (ManagedBackendSession.ensureConnected)
- packages/windie-sdk-js/src/transport/ManagedBackendSession.cjs:90-105 (ManagedBackendSession.connect)
- packages/windie-sdk-js/src/transport/ManagedBackendSession.cjs:184-202 (ManagedBackendSession.ensureConnected)

The open handler marks the connection as opened before buildHandshake/send succeeds. If buildHandshake or send throws, waiters are rejected, but the socket is not closed or cleared and opened remains true. Later ensureConnected only checks isOpen and returns true, so callers can send messages over a backend websocket that never received the handshake.

recommendation:
Track handshake completion separately from socket open state. If handshake construction or send fails, close and clear the socket, notify onSocketChange, and ensure later ensureConnected does not succeed until a handshake has completed.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for this feature, and the excerpt does not show coverage for handshake build/send failure or subsequent ensureConnected behavior.

suggested regression test:
Add ManagedBackendSession tests where buildHandshake throws and where socket.send throws during handshake; assert the socket is closed/cleared, connect waiters reject, and a later ensureConnected attempts a fresh connection instead of treating the stale open socket as ready.

minimum fix scope:
Update ManagedBackendSession.ts and its CommonJS counterpart or generation source so handshake failure closes/clears the socket and distinguishes open transport from completed session handshake.

repro:
Use a WebSocket implementation that reaches readyState 1 and emits open, and provide buildHandshake that throws. The first ensureConnected rejects, but the underlying open socket remains assigned; a subsequent ensureConnected returns true because isOpen is true even though no handshake completed.

## medium: Hydrating messages does not rebuild turn-to-conversation routing

id: fnd_sig-feat-service-37c269c6cc-3919_7a6a611272
category: api-contract
confidence: medium
triage: contract-mismatch
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/stores (feat_service_37c269c6cc)

The store keeps a global turnConversationRefs index used to resolve later turn-scoped events to a conversation. addMessage and updateMessage maintain that index, but setMessages replaces a workspace's full message list without indexing any message turnRefs. Any caller that hydrates or restores a conversation with setMessages will display the messages but leave resolveConversationRefForTurn unable to resolve their turns, so subsequent turn-scoped updates can fail to find the intended workspace.

recommendation:
When setMessages receives a concrete normalized conversationRef, derive normalized turn refs from the provided messages and merge them into turnConversationRefs. If setMessages is intentionally display-only, split that API from a hydration API and document the difference with tests.

test analysis:
No tests were linked for this feature, so the index-maintenance contract is not exercised across addMessage, updateMessage, and setMessages.

suggested regression test:
Add a store test that calls setMessages with messages containing turnRef for a non-active conversation and verifies resolveConversationRefForTurn maps those turns to that conversation.

minimum fix scope:
Extend setMessages to update turnConversationRefs for message.turnRef values when the target conversation can be normalized.

repro:
Call setMessages([{ id: 'm1', text: 'x', sender: 'assistant', turnRef: 'turn-1' }], 'conv-a'), then resolveConversationRefForTurn('turn-1'); it returns null because setMessages only updates the workspace messages array.

## medium: Import-time sys.path prepend can shadow backend modules globally

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-405798b7bc-5a49_879034f5ed
category: build-release

triage: risk

- backend/src/tools/browser/schema_types.py:5-9

Importing either browser_control_args_schema or schema_types mutates process-global import resolution by inserting frontend/src/main/python at the front of sys.path. Because this happens at module import time and uses index 0, any later import in the backend process can resolve to a same-named module from the sidecar-visible frontend tree before the backend environment or installed packages. That creates order-dependent backend behavior and release hazards that are hard to diagnose, especially in pytest where importing this feature can affect unrelated tests in the same interpreter.

recommendation:
Avoid process-global path prepending for the backend import path. Prefer packaging the shared contract as an explicit importable module for both backend and sidecar, or load this specific contract through a bounded import mechanism that does not alter global import precedence. If sys.path mutation is kept temporarily, append rather than prepend and constrain it behind a compatibility note with deletion plan.

test analysis:
No linked tests were provided for this feature, and the included files show no guard that verifies imports after this module cannot be shadowed by frontend/src/main/python.

suggested regression test:
Add a backend import-order test that imports backend.src.tools.browser.browser_control_args_schema, then verifies a deliberately conflicting module name resolves from the backend or environment path rather than frontend/src/main/python.

minimum fix scope:
Change shared_contract_loader.ensure_frontend_python_path and the import strategy used by browser_control_args_schema.py and schema_types.py.

## medium: In-flight session sync can restore a connected snapshot after disconnect

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-15a846a04c-60e8_5f1e69e52f
category: concurrency
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/runtime (feat_library_15a846a04c)

evidence:
- frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/runtime/browserSessionStore.js:180-237 (syncBrowserSession)
- frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/runtime/browserSessionStore.js:359-371 (disconnectBrowserSession)

syncBrowserSession only invalidates older syncs when another sync starts, then applies a connected snapshot after both status and get_tabs complete. disconnectBrowserSession closes the browser and applies a disconnected snapshot, but it does not invalidate any already-running sync request. If a poll sync started just before disconnect and finishes afterward with stale connected data, it can apply lines 227-237 and put the UI back into a connected state even though close already succeeded.

recommendation:
Invalidate pending syncs before state-changing actions such as disconnect, for example by incrementing syncRequestId at the start of disconnectBrowserSession or by using a dedicated generation token shared by sync and mutations. After close succeeds, ignore any sync result that began before the disconnect generation.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for this feature, and the package test script only shows a general Jest entrypoint without evidence of race coverage for this store.

suggested regression test:
Add a Jest test that stubs IpcBridge.invoke so a status/get_tabs sync is paused, calls disconnectBrowserSession while it is in flight, then resolves the stale get_tabs call and asserts getBrowserSessionSnapshot().connected remains false.

minimum fix scope:
frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/runtime/browserSessionStore.js plus a focused renderer store test.

repro:
Have a connected session with polling active. Let syncBrowserSession start and complete the status request, then trigger disconnectBrowserSession before get_tabs resolves. If get_tabs resolves with the pre-close tab list after disconnect applies its disconnected snapshot, the older sync can reapply connected=true.

## medium: In-flight successes can reopen a failed provider circuit

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-01d26df760-e150_9e1a1bdb15
category: concurrency
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/core/inference (feat_library_01d26df760)

evidence:
- backend/src/core/inference/circuit_breaker.py:83-90 (ProviderCircuitBreaker.record_success / record_failure)
- backend/src/core/inference/embedding_router.py:129-151 (EmbeddingRouter.embed_text)
- backend/src/core/inference/ocr_router.py:181-202 (OcrRouter.analyze_image)
- backend/src/core/inference/vision_router.py:191-212 (VisionRouter.predict_coordinates)
- backend/src/core/inference/vision_router.py:236-257 (VisionRouter.answer_question_about_image)

Each router checks the circuit before awaiting provider work, then records success or failure after the await. The circuit breaker has a single mutable state where record_success unconditionally resets all failures and opened_until. With concurrent requests, several failures can open the circuit while an older request is still in flight; if that older request later succeeds, record_success resets the opened circuit and immediately allows new traffic before the cooldown expires. This defeats the repeated-failure protection under realistic async concurrency.

recommendation:
Track whether a success belongs to the current circuit generation before allowing it to reset an opened circuit, or protect the breaker with request epochs so successes that began before the circuit opened cannot clear opened_until. At minimum, do not let record_success clear an already-open circuit unless it is part of an explicit probe/recovery path.

test analysis:
No tests are included for this feature, and there is no concurrent request scenario covering out-of-order provider completions.

suggested regression test:
Add an async concurrency test using a controllable fake provider where two failed calls open the circuit and a delayed success from a request started earlier must not clear circuit_open before cooldown.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/core/inference/circuit_breaker.py and focused async router or circuit breaker regression tests.

repro:
Set failure_threshold=2. Start three concurrent router calls while the circuit is closed. Let two fail first so record_failure opens the circuit, then let the earlier in-flight call succeed. Its record_success resets opened_until, making circuit_open false during the intended cooldown.

## medium: Initialization lock is shared across threads as an asyncio.Lock

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-eb603057ae-e315_28b7928c8c
category: concurrency
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/core/bootstrap (feat_library_eb603057ae)

evidence:
- backend/src/core/bootstrap/coordinator.py:52-55 (InitializationCoordinator.__init__)
- backend/src/core/bootstrap/coordinator.py:93-102 (InitializationCoordinator.initialize)

The coordinator documents thread-safe initialization, but it creates one asyncio.Lock and then awaits it from initialize(). The threading.Lock only serializes creation of the asyncio.Lock; it does not make acquiring that asyncio.Lock safe across different threads or event loops. If the same InitializationCoordinator is initialized concurrently from two threads using separate event loops, the shared asyncio.Lock can fail with event-loop affinity errors or fail to provide the intended cross-thread exclusion. That means the advertised protection against multiple concurrent initializations is incomplete exactly in the cross-thread case called out by the comments.

recommendation:
Use a real cross-thread gate for the whole initialization decision, such as a threading.Lock around a single in-flight initialization future/task owned by one loop, or explicitly make the coordinator single-event-loop only and remove the cross-thread safety claim. If cross-thread calls are supported, serialize entry before touching asyncio primitives and return/raise a deterministic result to losing callers.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for this feature, and the affected path requires concurrent calls from separate threads/event loops rather than ordinary single-loop async tests.

suggested regression test:
Add a test that calls initialize() concurrently from two threads with separate event loops and asserts one initialization succeeds while the other fails deterministically with the documented already-initialized error, without asyncio loop-affinity errors.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/core/bootstrap/coordinator.py initialization locking strategy and a focused concurrency regression test.

repro:
Create one InitializationCoordinator, start two OS threads, and call asyncio.run(coordinator.initialize(...)) in both threads. Depending on timing and loop binding, one call can raise an asyncio lock/event-loop error instead of the intended serialized initialization/RuntimeError path.

## medium: Install auth token is persisted without explicit restrictive file permissions

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-a3814e83b5-f2bc_b7764062a4
category: security
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/main/ipc/:ipc#1 (feat_library_a3814e83b5)

evidence:
- frontend/src/main/ipc/ipc_install_auth_state.cjs:21-35 (normalizeInstallAuthState)
- frontend/src/main/ipc/ipc_install_auth_state.cjs:57-68 (saveInstallAuthStateToDisk)

The persisted install auth state includes installToken, userId, and installId, but saveInstallAuthStateToDisk writes the JSON file with default process umask permissions and no platform credential store or explicit mode. On machines or packaged environments with permissive umasks or shared userData directories, this can expose a reusable install credential to other local users or processes.

recommendation:
Persist install auth through the OS credential store where available, or at minimum write the file with restrictive permissions such as mode 0o600 and ensure the parent directory is not group/world-readable. Avoid logging token-bearing payloads and document the migration/security behavior.

test analysis:
No included tests assert filesystem mode, credential-store usage, or migration behavior for install-auth.json.

suggested regression test:
Add a focused test that saves install auth state using a temp userData path and asserts the resulting file mode is owner-read/write only on POSIX platforms, with an explicit Windows expectation if ACL checks are implemented separately.

minimum fix scope:
saveInstallAuthStateToDisk and associated install-auth persistence tests.

## medium: Instance-level last-usage fields are unsafe for overlapping provider requests

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-ed619aedb2-1b44_317497093d
category: concurrency
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/llm/providers/:base (feat_library_ed619aedb2)

evidence:
- backend/src/llm/providers/base.py:69-71 (LLMProvider.__init__)
- backend/src/llm/providers/base.py:255-275 (LLMProvider usage accessors)
- backend/src/llm/providers/base.py:299-317 (LLMProvider._record_usage_from_payload_container)
- backend/src/llm/providers/base.py:451-476 (LLMProvider stream helpers)

Usage and normalized stream response payloads are stored as mutable state on the provider object. The stream helpers pass bound self recorders into the streaming pipeline, so two concurrent streams on the same provider instance can overwrite each other's _last_usage, _last_stream_usage, or _last_stream_response_payload. Downstream code that reads these accessors after a stream may attach the wrong usage or tool-call payload to a turn.

recommendation:
Move usage and stream response payload capture into request-local state returned by the streaming pipeline, or key the stored payload by request/turn id. If global last-value accessors must remain, document them as diagnostics only and avoid using them for turn-critical behavior.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included, and ordinary single-stream tests would not expose cross-request overwrites.

suggested regression test:
Add an async test that runs two fake streams concurrently through one provider instance and asserts each request observes only its own usage/payload state.

minimum fix scope:
Refactor stream usage/payload capture so the active stream owns its state rather than writing request-critical metadata to shared provider fields.

repro:
Start two get_completion_stream calls concurrently on one provider instance, with stream A receiving a usage chunk after stream B. After stream B finishes and reads get_last_stream_usage(), stream A can overwrite the shared provider field before or during that read.

## medium: Invalid mouse method config is treated as an empty allowlist instead of being ignored

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-5a46c3e63b-c9c2_2f7a3c2d47
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/tools/:tool (feat_library_5a46c3e63b)

evidence:
- backend/src/tools/tool_selection.py:387-415 (_parse_selection)
- backend/src/tools/tool_selection.py:77-89 (ToolSelection.get_allowed_mouse_coordinate_methods)
- backend/src/tools/tool_selection.py:470-483 (should_initialize_ocr/should_initialize_vision)
- tests/backend/test_tool_policy.py:484-498 (test_should_initialize_startup_services_follow_mouse_methods)

When tool_options.mouse_control.enabled_coordinate_methods is present but not an array, the parser logs that it is ignoring the invalid value, but still assigns mouse_methods to an empty frozenset. Downstream, an empty frozenset means mouse_control has no allowed coordinate methods, which disables mouse_control and prevents OCR/vision initialization. That contradicts the warning's stated behavior and makes a malformed dev config disable local desktop capability rather than falling back to the default of all coordinate methods. The existing startup-service test covers an explicit valid manual-only list, not the malformed-config path.

recommendation:
Only assign mouse_methods when enabled_coordinate_methods is a valid list/tuple/set, or leave it as None for malformed values so the default all-methods behavior is preserved. If fail-closed behavior is intentional, change the warning and add an explicit test documenting that invalid config disables all coordinate methods.

test analysis:
The included tests exercise valid coordinate-method lists and the resulting OCR/vision startup gating, but do not cover a malformed enabled_coordinate_methods value despite the parser having a specific invalid-value branch.

suggested regression test:
Add a test that loads a config with [tool_options.mouse_control] enabled_coordinate_methods = "manual" and asserts get_allowed_mouse_coordinate_methods() returns manual, ocr, and prediction, and should_initialize_ocr()/should_initialize_vision() remain true.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/tools/tool_selection.py _parse_selection plus one focused backend test in tests/backend/test_tool_policy.py or a dedicated tool_selection test.

## medium: Lazy service initialization can replace in-memory run state under concurrent first requests

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-bcaf4a766a-ec47_3f8a93633e
category: concurrency
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/api/routes/runs (feat_library_bcaf4a766a)

evidence:
- backend/src/api/routes/runs/support.py:23-33 (get_vm_run_control_service)
- backend/src/api/routes/runs/router.py:44-61 (create_run)
- backend/src/api/routes/runs/router.py:83-89 (get_run)

get_vm_run_control_service lazily creates and stores the singleton on request.app.state without synchronization. If two first requests observe no service at the same time, both can construct separate VmRunControlService instances and whichever assignment happens last becomes the app-wide service. Any run created or event appended through the losing instance can then be unavailable to later requests, because later routes read from the overwritten instance. The impact depends on the ASGI concurrency model and service storage behavior, so confidence is medium, but the race is present in the route helper as shown.

recommendation:
Initialize VmRunControlService during app startup, or protect lazy initialization with an app-level lock so only one instance can be created and published. Prefer startup initialization because it also makes configuration failures explicit.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included, and there is no evidence of a concurrent first-request regression test around service initialization.

suggested regression test:
Add a focused async test that invokes the dependency concurrently on a fresh app state and asserts all callers receive the same VmRunControlService instance and that a run created through one caller is visible through the final stored service.

minimum fix scope:
support.py service dependency initialization path and one concurrency-focused backend test.

## medium: Length-prefixed stdout frame can grow result buffer without bounds

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-9d744ac113-cd58_42f8e3ba94
category: performance
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/main/:wakeword (feat_library_9d744ac113)

evidence:
- frontend/src/main/wakeword_bridge.cjs:221-230
- frontend/src/main/wakeword_bridge.cjs:232-265

processDetectionResults trusts the 32-bit length prefix from the Python child and keeps buffering until 4 + length bytes arrive. A faulty or compromised wakeword service can send a very large length and then stream arbitrary data or stop, causing the Electron main process to retain an ever-growing resultBuffer with no maximum frame size, reset, or process error. Because this runs in the main process, it can degrade or crash the desktop app.

recommendation:
Define a maximum wakeword result frame size appropriate for the JSON messages, reject frames above that size, clear the buffer, emit/log an error, and consider stopping the child process when the framing protocol is violated.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for malformed stdout framing, oversized result frames, or buffer reset behavior after protocol violations.

suggested regression test:
Add a processDetectionResults-focused test that sends a length prefix above the configured maximum and verifies the buffer is cleared, no detection is emitted, and an error path is taken.

minimum fix scope:
Add frame-size validation in processDetectionResults and cover malformed length-prefixed stdout input.

## medium: Manual drag-destination normalization can be skipped for destination-only tools

## medium: MCP tool registry keeps stale registrations after rediscovery

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-4f3ef7d33a-8c5c_6f42f0e25b
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/main#1 (feat_library_4f3ef7d33a)

evidence:
- frontend/src/main/mcp_runtime.cjs:282-307 (normalizeDiscoveredTool)
- frontend/src/main/mcp_runtime.cjs:310-339 (discoverMcpTools)
- frontend/src/main/mcp_runtime.cjs:385-399 (executeMcpTool)

normalizeDiscoveredTool writes every discovered tool into the module-level toolRegistry, but discoverMcpTools never clears registrations for servers or tools that are no longer present. executeMcpTool only rediscoveres when the requested name is missing; if a tool was previously discovered and then removed or disabled from the MCP server configuration, the stale registry entry remains executable against the old cached server registration. That can expose disabled extension tools until cache clear or app restart.

recommendation:
Make discovery reconcile registry state with the current server specs. For example, remove all registry entries for the affected MCP server before repopulating, or rebuild toolRegistry from the complete discovered set. DisabledTools filtering in buildClientToolManifestWithMcp should also prevent stale execution if executeMcpTool can be reached by name.

test analysis:
No linked tests exercise MCP tool removal, disabled server behavior, or registry reconciliation across rediscovery.

suggested regression test:
Add a test that discovers a tool, changes the configured MCP servers to remove or disable it, runs discovery again, and asserts hasDiscoveredMcpTool is false and executeMcpTool returns null.

minimum fix scope:
frontend/src/main/mcp_runtime.cjs registry lifecycle handling in discoverMcpTools/executeMcpTool, plus focused MCP runtime tests.

repro:
Discover an MCP server that exposes tool A, then remove or disable that server/tool and call executeMcpTool('A') without clearMcpRuntimeCache. Since toolRegistry still has A, executeMcpTool skips rediscovery and calls the stale registration.

## medium: Mixed bundle IDs are treated as one atomic bundle

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-a789bd11e1-69f1_7334d05e01
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/agent/tools/shared (feat_library_a789bd11e1)

evidence:
- backend/src/agent/tools/shared/bundle_detection.py:14-32 (is_atomic_bundle)
- backend/src/agent/tools/shared/bundle_detection.py:35-56 (is_atomic_bundle_from_results)
- tests/backend/test_bundle_detection.py:29-46 (test_is_atomic_bundle_requires_bundle_id_and_no_request_id)

Both bundle detectors only require every call/result to have bundle metadata and no request metadata. They do not verify that all entries belong to the same bundle ID. That means two unrelated bundle fragments with different bundle_id values in the same parsed response or result list can be classified as a single atomic bundle, which can collapse unrelated execution results into one atomic narrative or cause orchestration logic to apply bundle semantics to mixed groups. The existing test covers matching bundle IDs and request_id exclusion, but not mismatched bundle IDs.

recommendation:
Track the first bundle execution reference or bundle_id and require every subsequent tool call/result to match it before returning true.

test analysis:
The included detection tests only use bundle_id="b1" for all positive examples and never pass multiple distinct bundle IDs.

suggested regression test:
Add cases where two ParsedToolCall objects have different bundle_id values and assert both is_atomic_bundle and is_atomic_bundle_from_results return False.

minimum fix scope:
Update bundle_detection.py to compare bundle identity across calls/results and extend tests/backend/test_bundle_detection.py with mismatched-bundle cases.

## medium: Nested list/dict reasoning payloads are dropped inside dict fields

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-1bff7eeb5f-0445_c94c3e865d
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/llm/providers#2 (feat_library_1bff7eeb5f)

evidence:
- backend/src/llm/providers/thinking_extraction.py:30-56 (_extract_string_content)
- backend/src/llm/providers/thinking_extraction.py:69-77 (_extract_thinking_from_content_blocks)

_extract_string_content claims to extract strings from list and dict payload shapes, but when it handles a dict it only returns nested values that are already strings. It does not recurse into dict fields whose values are lists or nested dicts. As a result, a reasoning content block such as {"type":"reasoning","content":[{"text":"..."}]} or {"type":"thinking","reasoning_details":[...]} reaches _extract_string_content(block) and returns None even though the same helper can extract from a list when the list is passed directly. Providers often wrap reasoning text in content/details arrays, so these thinking deltas can be silently omitted from the stream.

recommendation:
In the dict branch of _extract_string_content, call _extract_string_content(nested) for each supported key and return the first non-empty result, instead of accepting only direct strings.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for this feature, and the excerpt has no regression coverage for structured reasoning blocks with nested list or dict content.

suggested regression test:
Add a unit test for extract_thinking_content with a delta containing content=[{"type":"reasoning","content":[{"text":"hidden reasoning"}]}] and assert it returns "hidden reasoning".

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/llm/providers/thinking_extraction.py

## medium: OCR inspect masks unexpected resolver failures as normal resolution errors

## medium: OpenAI websocket can leak if session update fails during connect

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-9a79195fcc-eace_952a0b4cfd
category: api-contract
confidence: medium
triage: contract-mismatch
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/api/services/transcription (feat_library_9a79195fcc)

evidence:
- backend/src/api/services/transcription/openai_realtime.py:46-52 (OpenAIRealtimeTranscriptionSession.connect)
- backend/src/api/services/transcription/openai_realtime.py:164-195 (OpenAIRealtimeTranscriptionSession._apply_session_update)
- backend/src/api/services/transcription/openai_realtime.py:197-199 (OpenAIRealtimeTranscriptionSession._send_json)

connect stores the websocket on self._connection and then awaits _apply_session_update. If the session.update send fails, connect raises with self._connection still set and the websocket not closed. Callers that treat a failed connect as no active session may not call close, leaving a provider connection open and leaving subsequent calls pointed at a half-initialized session.

recommendation:
Wrap the post-connect session update in try/except; on failure, close the newly opened connection, clear self._connection, then re-raise the original exception.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for connection setup failure paths, and the code has no local guard around the post-connect initialization step.

suggested regression test:
Add a unit test that simulates _apply_session_update/send failure after websockets.connect succeeds and asserts the connection is closed and self._connection is cleared.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/api/services/transcription/openai_realtime.py connect error cleanup plus a focused backend unit test.

repro:
Mock websockets.connect to return a connection whose send raises during _apply_session_update; await connect and observe that close was not called and self._connection remains assigned.

## medium: Packaged sidecar launch crashes for extensionless service names

## medium: Paste-region update ignores replaced selection length

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-5a0a2d308d-fdad_6d98b1dbf2
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/utils#2 (feat_library_5a0a2d308d)

evidence:
- frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/utils/transcriptionRegions.ts:76-87 (buildValueAfterPaste)
- frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/utils/transcriptionRegions.ts:90-116 (updateRegionAfterPaste)

The paste value builder supports replacing an arbitrary selection using both selectionStart and selectionEnd, but the region updater receives only cursorPosition and pastedTextLength. When a paste replaces selected text before or across the active transcription region, the active region is shifted by the inserted length only and never accounts for the removed selection span. For example, if the active region starts at 10 and the user replaces characters 0-5 with a 2-character paste, the value shrinks by 3 but updateRegionAfterPaste shifts the region forward by 2, leaving stale offsets that point at the wrong text. If the selection overlaps the active region, the updater can also preserve a region that should be cleared.

recommendation:
Pass the full paste selection range into updateRegionAfterPaste and update/clear the region based on the net length delta and overlap with the selected range. Shift only when the replacement range is entirely before the region, preserve only when entirely after, and clear or recompute when it overlaps the region.

test analysis:
No tests are listed for this feature, and the included package scripts show Jest is available but no linked test covers these region edge cases.

suggested regression test:
Add Jest coverage for paste replacement before an active transcription region and paste replacement overlapping an active region, asserting the first shifts by pastedTextLength - removedLength and the second clears the region.

minimum fix scope:
Update transcriptionRegions.ts paste-region API and its call sites/tests to include selectionEnd or removed length.

## medium: Payloads with explicit null error are marked as failures

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-9d91fd1086-c4e9_f089ef2804

ToolResult.from_payload infers success from the presence of the error key, not from whether the error value is meaningful. A mapping-shaped tool payload that includes a stable field set such as {"error": null, "data": ...} will be normalized with success=false even though there is no error. That can make successful SDK/local-runtime ingress results look failed whenever callers include nullable error fields for schema stability.

recommendation:

test analysis:

suggested regression test:
