minimum fix scope:
backend/src/core/interfaces/tool.py and a focused backend unit test for ToolResult.from_payload normalization.

repro:
Call ToolResult.from_payload({"error": None, "data": {"output": "ok"}}); the returned ToolResult has success=False.

## medium: Per-instance mutable response state is unsafe for overlapping LLM turns

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-38cf037009-fa72_09225344c7
category: concurrency
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/agent/llm (feat_library_38cf037009)

evidence:
- backend/src/agent/llm/llm_stream_processor.py:68-70 (LLMStreamProcessor.__init__)
- backend/src/agent/llm/llm_stream_processor.py:93-114 (LLMStreamProcessor.get_response)
- backend/src/agent/llm/llm_stream_processor.py:127-164 (LLMStreamProcessor.get_response)
- backend/src/agent/llm/llm_stream_processor.py:207-211 (LLMStreamProcessor.get_last_response_payload)
- backend/src/agent/llm/llm_stream_processor.py:281-298 (LLMStreamProcessor._resolve_previous_response_id)

The processor stores turn-local data such as _last_response_payload and prompt fingerprints on the shared processor instance. get_response clears _last_response_payload at the start of every call, then later writes the result payload; _resolve_previous_response_id also reads that shared field to wire OpenAI tool-result continuations. If two get_response generators overlap on the same processor, one turn can clear or overwrite another turn's payload, causing get_last_response_payload to return the wrong tool calls or causing an OpenAI tool-result turn to miss/use the wrong previous_response_id. This is a concrete async race because get_response awaits provider streaming between reads and writes of shared mutable state.

recommendation:
Keep turn-local response payload in a local variable returned or yielded through the response event path, or serialize get_response calls per processor/session with an async lock. For OpenAI previous_response_id continuation, store continuation state under an explicit conversation/tool-call key instead of a single shared last-payload slot.

test analysis:
All included tests collect one response at a time from a processor instance; none overlap two get_response async generators or read get_last_response_payload while another turn is in flight.

suggested regression test:
Add an asyncio test with a controlled fake client that blocks mid-stream, starts a second get_response call on the same processor, then resumes both and asserts each caller sees its own payload and previous_response_id state.

minimum fix scope:
LLMStreamProcessor response-state ownership and focused async concurrency coverage

repro:
Start two tasks that iterate the same LLMStreamProcessor.get_response instance concurrently, with one tool-call turn producing a response_id/tool_calls and a second turn starting before the first caller reads get_last_response_payload. The second turn sets _last_response_payload to None at line 114 and can later overwrite it, so the first caller can observe None or the second turn's payload.

minimum fix scope:

## medium: Refreshing overrides does not refresh already-created singleton handlers

category: bug

## medium: Release packaging can ship without TypeScript or lint validation

id: fnd_sig-feat-release-36a60cc78c-31c3_0aa724c3ec
category: build-release
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open

evidence:
- frontend/package.json:17-21
- frontend/package.json:26-27
- frontend/package.json:40-41

The release packaging scripts all invoke `npm run build`, but `build` is only `vite build`. Vite's production build generally transpiles TypeScript and bundles assets, while the explicit `typecheck`, `lint`, and Jest test scripts are separate and not part of the package path. A package command can therefore produce distributable artifacts even when type errors, lint-enforced release blockers, or frontend test regressions exist.

recommendation:
Make the release packaging path depend on a validation script, for example `npm run typecheck && npm run lint && npm run test:ci && vite build`, or introduce a dedicated `prepackage`/`release:check` script used by all package variants before `electron-builder`.

test analysis:
No included test or package-script assertion covers the command graph for release packaging; the only evidence shows validation scripts exist but are not wired into `build` or `package`.

suggested regression test:
Add a small package-script contract test that parses `frontend/package.json` and asserts every release packaging script runs the required validation script before `electron-builder`.

minimum fix scope:
Update `frontend/package.json` scripts so package/release commands run frontend typecheck, lint, and CI tests before producing distributable artifacts.

## medium: Remote provider ignores its not-ready state when analyzing images

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-5268c1cca5-45c7_93b4ba26e8
category: api-contract
confidence: medium
triage: contract-mismatch
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/services/ocr (feat_library_5268c1cca5)

evidence:
- backend/src/services/ocr/remote_provider.py:43-53 (RemoteHttpOcrProvider.enabled)
- backend/src/services/ocr/remote_provider.py:68-93 (RemoteHttpOcrProvider.health_check)
- backend/src/services/ocr/remote_provider.py:95-103 (RemoteHttpOcrProvider.analyze_image)

The provider advertises enabled/is_ready as false when health checks have marked _ready false, but analyze_image gates only on _enabled and still sends requests to the remote OCR endpoint. Callers that rely on enabled or is_ready to represent provider availability can observe the provider as unavailable while analyze_image continues to make network calls and potentially mutate _ready back to true. That makes readiness semantics inconsistent and can route work to a provider that failed health.

recommendation:
Either make analyze_image enforce readiness by checking self.enabled/is_ready and raising ProviderRequestError when not ready, or explicitly document and rename the readiness semantics so failed health is advisory and request success may recover the provider.

test analysis:
The included test file covers only local OcrService helpers and CUDA fallback behavior; it has no RemoteHttpOcrProvider readiness or health-failure tests.

suggested regression test:
Add a remote provider test with a mocked client where health_check times out, then assert analyze_image does not issue a POST and raises a ProviderRequestError while _ready is false.

minimum fix scope:
Update RemoteHttpOcrProvider.analyze_image readiness guard and add focused tests for health failure followed by analyze_image.

repro:
After health_check catches a timeout and sets _ready=False, enabled and is_ready both return False. A subsequent analyze_image call still posts to /ocr/analyze because it checks only _enabled.

If the SDK runtime generated a turn ref for the local optimistic turn, the backend has no way to echo it through this IPC path, so the renderer can fail to correlate stream events with the active turn or may fail open in stale-turn checks.

recommendation:
Include the normalized turn_ref in the query IPC payload whenever the SDK payload contains turn_ref or turnRef, and ensure backend event echoes preserve that value.

## medium: Resolved calls are stored by reference despite the immutable-copy contract

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-d1b39f615f-7e6c_cd207fbb1a
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/agent/tools/preparation/storage (feat_library_d1b39f615f)

evidence:
- backend/src/agent/tools/preparation/storage/resolved_call_storage.py:14-16 (ResolvedToolCallStorage)
- backend/src/agent/tools/preparation/storage/resolved_call_storage.py:23-34 (ResolvedToolCallStorage.register)
- backend/src/agent/tools/preparation/storage/resolved_call_storage.py:36-49 (ResolvedToolCallStorage.get)

The class contract says resolved tool calls are immutable copies used later by ToolOrchestrator during execution, but register stores the provided object directly and get returns the same object. If the caller keeps and mutates the original parsed/resolved call after registration, or mutates the object returned by get, the stored execution payload changes behind the storage boundary. That can corrupt resolved coordinates or arguments for later execution.

recommendation:
Copy or freeze resolved_call at the storage boundary, and avoid returning the mutable stored object directly. If resolved calls are dataclasses or Pydantic models, use the appropriate model copy/freeze API; otherwise use a structured deep copy for plain dict/list payloads.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for this feature, and the current excerpt has no test coverage asserting copy isolation or immutability.

suggested regression test:
Register a mutable resolved call, mutate the original and a retrieved value, then assert a subsequent get still returns the originally registered resolved arguments.

minimum fix scope:
Update ResolvedToolCallStorage.register/get to enforce snapshot semantics for the supported resolved-call payload type and add focused storage tests.

## medium: Responses tool conversion can emit invalid function tools from chat-shaped specs

id: fnd_sig-feat-service-8483747583-4866_4cc00fbd5b
category: api-contract
confidence: medium
triage: contract-mismatch
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/llm/providers/:openai (feat_service_8483747583)

evidence:
- backend/src/llm/providers/openai.py:53-70 (OpenAIProvider.get_completion)
- backend/src/llm/providers/openai.py:83-101 (OpenAIProvider._stream_internal)
- backend/src/llm/providers/openai_tool_prep.py:126-145 (build_openai_responses_tools)

The Responses path passes request tools directly into build_openai_responses_params, which ultimately calls build_openai_responses_tools. That converter emits Responses function tools from top-level tool.get('name'), tool.get('description'), and tool.get('parameters'). OpenAI/LiteLLM chat tool specs are commonly shaped as {'type':'function','function':{name,description,parameters}}, and this provider's non-Responses path explicitly handles that nested shape through make_openai_chat_tools_compatible. If the same chat-shaped tools reach the Responses runtime, the emitted function tool can have name=None, description=None, and default empty parameters, causing OpenAI request rejection or loss of the actual callable schema.

recommendation:
Normalize both flat Windie tool specs and OpenAI chat-shaped function specs before emitting Responses tools. Extract name, description, parameters, and strict from tool['function'] when present, and add a focused unit test for chat-shaped tools in the Responses path.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for this feature, and the excerpt shows no assertion that Responses tool conversion preserves nested chat function metadata.

suggested regression test:
Add a test that passes [{'type':'function','function':{'name':'click','description':'Click','parameters':{'type':'object','properties':{'x':{'type':'integer'}}}}}] into build_openai_responses_tools and asserts the output function has name 'click' and the original parameters.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/llm/providers/openai_tool_prep.py plus a focused provider/tool-prep test.

## medium: Restore failure can leave screenshot capture listeners stuck active

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-7647eac87f-877c_a924806bec
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/services (feat_library_7647eac87f)

evidence:
- frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/services/ScreenshotAttachmentPipeline.ts:220-241 (captureScreenshotAttachment)

The finally block awaits `restoreScreenshotCaptureVisibility` before emitting `active:false` and before logging timing. If the restore call rejects, JavaScript exits the finally block immediately, so listeners never receive the inactive event and the function rejects instead of returning the empty or captured attachment. That makes a cleanup failure cascade into stale UI/capture state.

recommendation:
Wrap restore in its own try/catch inside finally, record/log the restore error, then always dispatch the inactive event and timing log. If callers must know restore failed, return the attachment while logging or rethrow only after cleanup notifications have run.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for cleanup failure paths, and the current structure has no evidence of assertions around restore rejection behavior.

suggested regression test:
Add a test where capture succeeds but `restoreScreenshotCaptureVisibility` rejects; assert `active:false` is still dispatched and timing/log cleanup still runs.

minimum fix scope:
ScreenshotAttachmentPipeline.ts finally-block cleanup ordering plus one focused frontend test.

repro:
Mock `restoreScreenshotCaptureVisibility` to reject during `captureScreenshotAttachment()`. The `windie:screenshot-capture` active=false event is skipped because it is after the awaited restore call in the same finally block.

## medium: Retry path drops assistant and tool transcript metadata

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-f0fc56ef53-3633_c12b0d8906
category: data-loss
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/app/runtime#2 (feat_library_f0fc56ef53)

evidence:
- frontend/src/renderer/app/runtime/desktopTranscriptProjectionRuntimeClient.ts:182-189
- frontend/src/renderer/app/runtime/desktopTranscriptProjectionRuntimeClient.ts:194-205
- frontend/src/renderer/app/runtime/desktopTranscriptProjectionRuntimeClient.ts:219-229
- frontend/src/renderer/app/runtime/desktopTranscriptProjectionRuntimeClient.ts:234-247

The immediate assistant entry persists timestamp and structuredPayload, but the queued retry options omit both fields; the tool retry options omit timestamp even though the immediate tool entry persists it. When persistProjectionEntry rejects, queueForRetry stores only retryOptions, so a later retry can recreate a transcript entry without the original metadata. That corrupts transcript ordering/audit details specifically on the failure path.

recommendation:
Include the same metadata in retryOptions that buildEntry writes for the immediate entry, at least timestamp for assistant/tool entries and structuredPayload for assistant entries. Keep the immediate and retry entry construction paths parity-tested.

test analysis:
The feature metadata lists no linked tests, and the excerpt shows no test coverage for failed persistence followed by pending-message retry metadata parity.

suggested regression test:
Add a focused renderer/runtime test that mocks persistence failure, records assistant/tool messages with timestamp and structuredPayload, flushes pending messages after session resolution, and asserts the retried stored entry preserves the same metadata as the immediate build path.

minimum fix scope:
Update desktopTranscriptProjectionRuntimeClient retryOptions for assistant/tool messages and add a focused retry-path parity test.

repro:
Force persistProjectionEntry/storeTranscriptEntry to reject while calling recordAssistantMessage with timestamp and structuredPayload, or recordToolMessage with timestamp; then flush pending messages and inspect the stored retried transcript entry metadata.

## medium: Root logging profile changes can be skipped after any handler is installed

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-f459ec7214-5664_73b2400a06
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/core (feat_library_f459ec7214)

evidence:
- backend/src/core/logging_setup.py:102-117 (configure_logging)
- tests/backend/test_logging_setup.py:12-48

configure_logging computes the intended root level from the selected profile or LOG_LEVEL, but applies it only through logging.basicConfig. In Python, basicConfig is a no-op once the root logger already has handlers, which is common when a server, test harness, or embedding host initializes logging first. In that case the profile-specific root level and format are silently not applied, while the function still proceeds to mutate selected named loggers, leaving logging in a partially configured state. The included tests cover constants and the LiteLLM helper, but they do not exercise configure_logging with a pre-existing root handler, so this regression would pass.

recommendation:
Make configure_logging explicitly set the root logger level and either pass force=True to basicConfig where acceptable or update existing handlers intentionally. If preserving existing handlers is required, document that behavior and set only the root level directly.

test analysis:
The current tests import _IMPORTANT_PROFILE_LOGGERS and _configure_litellm_runtime_flags only; they never call configure_logging or simulate an already-configured root logger.

suggested regression test:
Add a test that attaches a temporary root handler, calls configure_logging(profile="verbose") with LOG_LEVEL unset, and asserts the root logger is set to DEBUG or that the intended handler policy is applied deterministically.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/core/logging_setup.py plus focused tests in tests/backend/test_logging_setup.py

repro:
Install any root handler before calling configure_logging, then call configure_logging(profile="verbose") and observe that the root logger level/format is not changed by logging.basicConfig.

## medium: Runtime config updates leave DI config consumers on the old AppConfig

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-628701dd9c-eafb_e35d5670ff
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/core/container (feat_library_628701dd9c)

evidence:
- backend/src/core/container/core_container.py:41-54
- backend/src/core/container/config_updater.py:47-84
- backend/src/core/container/facade.py:71-74
- backend/src/core/container/tool_container.py:59-67

CoreContainer.config is a singleton loaded once and used by factory providers such as llm_client and by the ToolContainer config dependency. update_config updates ConfigService/ConfigManager and manually refreshes selected facade references, but it does not reset or override the DI config provider. As a result, later calls through DI-backed factories can still receive the startup AppConfig even after Container.update_config succeeds; llm_client is one direct example because the facade property always calls _di_container.llm_client(), whose cfg argument is still core.config. New ToolResultOrchestrator instances can have the same stale-config problem through ToolContainer.config.

recommendation:
When applying updated_config, reset or override the core.config provider to providers.Object(updated_config), then invalidate or reset dependent config-scoped providers that should be recreated. Keep the existing manual refresh_runtime_config calls only for already-instantiated objects that cannot be refreshed through DI reset.

test analysis:
The included tests only cover incoming route validation and handler binding. They never instantiate Container, call update_config, or assert that DI-backed factories observe the updated AppConfig.

suggested regression test:
Add a backend container test that constructs Container with an initial ConfigManager, calls update_config with a distinguishable AppConfig value, and asserts that container.config, container.llm_client creation, and a newly created tool_orchestrator receive the updated config rather than the startup config.

minimum fix scope:
ContainerConfigUpdater.update_config and any directly dependent provider reset/invalidation needed for config consumers.

## medium: Screenshot restore bounds are discarded when the hidden main window has an active display affinity

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-2ce5f9322a-c127_ed68e89065
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/main/:window (feat_library_2ce5f9322a)

evidence:
- frontend/src/main/window_suppression_runtime.cjs:87-103 (rememberWindowBoundsForScreenshotSuppression / restoreWindowBoundsFromScreenshotSuppression)
- frontend/src/main/window_visibility_runtime.cjs:258-287 (showMainWindow)

Screenshot suppression records the pre-suppression bounds on the window, but showMainWindow resolves a target display affinity for any hidden main window via getActiveDisplayAffinity. When that value exists, the restore marker is deleted and the window is centered or fit on the active display instead of restoring the exact saved bounds. This means a hide/show cycle used only for screenshot capture can unexpectedly move or resize the main window.

recommendation:
Prioritize restoring __windieScreenshotRestoreBounds before resolving implicit display affinity, or only bypass restoration when the caller explicitly supplied targetDisplayAffinity or requested a display move.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for this feature, and the provided package manifest only exposes generic Jest commands, so there is no evidence that screenshot suppression restore behavior is covered.

suggested regression test:
Add a main-process unit test for hideMainWindow({ suppressForScreenshot: true }) followed by showMainWindow() with getActiveDisplayAffinity returning a display object, asserting the saved bounds are restored and not deleted before restoration.

minimum fix scope:
Change showMainWindow's ordering/condition around lines 258-287 so saved screenshot restore bounds win over implicit active display affinity unless a caller explicitly opts into repositioning.

repro:
Have the main window visible at a custom size/position, call hideMainWindow({ suppressForScreenshot: true }) while getActiveDisplayAffinity returns a display object, then call showMainWindow() without an explicit targetDisplayAffinity. The saved __windieScreenshotRestoreBounds is deleted and centerWindowOnDisplayWorkArea or fitWindowToDisplayWorkArea is used instead of restoreWindowBoundsFromScreenshotSuppression.

## medium: SDK debug routes can select another user's session config when no authenticated identity is present

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-333730a5a5-7471_56c314b458
category: security
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/api/routes/sdk (feat_library_333730a5a5)

evidence:
- backend/src/api/routes/sdk/router.py:357-374 (sdk_debug_models)
- backend/src/api/routes/sdk/router.py:444-465 (sdk_debug_system_prompt)
- backend/src/api/routes/sdk/router.py:468-490 (sdk_debug_prompt_preview)
- backend/src/api/routes/sdk/service.py:126-154 (resolve_effective_debug_config)

The debug routes accept a caller-supplied user_id and pass it to resolve_effective_debug_config. That helper uses the authenticated install identity only when present; otherwise it falls back to the supplied user_id and loads that user's session config. These endpoints return model config, tool schemas, system prompt, and constructed prompt data. If these SDK routes are reachable in any unauthenticated or weakly authenticated context, a client can enumerate or inspect another user's effective prompt/config by guessing a user_id.

recommendation:
Do not trust user_id query/body values for session selection on SDK debug endpoints. Require an authenticated identity for user-scoped session lookup, or restrict user_id override to privileged/internal callers with an explicit authorization check. If unauthenticated SDK debug access is intentionally allowed, ignore user_id and use only container/global config.

test analysis:
No tests were included for this feature, and there is no visible authorization test around user_id selection for the debug routes.

suggested regression test:
Add route/service tests that simulate no authenticated identity and a supplied user_id, asserting that user-scoped session config is not loaded unless the caller is authorized. Also test the authenticated identity case ignores conflicting user_id parameters.

minimum fix scope:
Tighten resolve_effective_debug_config or the debug route dependencies so caller-supplied user_id cannot select arbitrary sessions without authorization.

repro:
In an environment where get_current_authenticated_install_identity() returns None, call /api/sdk/system-prompt?user_id=<other-user> or /api/sdk/models?user_id=<other-user>. The helper attempts session_manager.get_session(<other-user>) and builds the response from that session config.

## medium: Shared pending TTS set can drop tasks created while waiting

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-cddaa1a4b0-10c8_e0017d7047
category: concurrency
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/api/processing (feat_library_cddaa1a4b0)

evidence:
- backend/src/api/processing/pipeline.py:49-55 (StreamPipeline.__init__)
- backend/src/api/processing/pipeline.py:119-125 (StreamPipeline.process)
- backend/src/api/processing/pipeline.py:143-147 (StreamPipeline.wait_for_pending_tts)

The pipeline stores pending TTS tasks in an instance-level set, then wait_for_pending_tts snapshots the current set, awaits only that snapshot, and unconditionally clears the whole set. If the same StreamPipeline instance processes another event while wait_for_pending_tts is awaiting, the new task can be added to _pending_tts_tasks after the snapshot and then removed by clear() without being awaited. A subsequent flush could therefore run before that TTS chunk finishes, reintroducing the audio-loss race this tracking is meant to prevent. The class comments also say all per-stream state should live outside the pipeline, but this set is pipeline-owned shared state.

recommendation:
Move pending TTS tracking to a per-stream processor/context, or make wait_for_pending_tts remove only the tasks it actually awaited, for example by discarding the snapshot tasks instead of clearing the entire shared set. If StreamPipeline is intended to be per-query, enforce that lifecycle at construction/use sites and document it in the contract.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for concurrent calls or for tasks added while wait_for_pending_tts is awaiting.

suggested regression test:
Create a fake TTS processor whose first task blocks, call wait_for_pending_tts, then call process again before the first task completes. Assert the second task remains pending or is awaited before flush is allowed.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/api/processing/pipeline.py

## medium: Sidecar discovery can redirect privileged daemon requests to arbitrary URLs

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-69aa448df6-9eb8_259405a852
category: security
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/main#2 (feat_library_69aa448df6)

evidence:
- frontend/src/main/sidecar_daemon_manager.cjs:24-41 (normalizeDiscovery)
- frontend/src/main/sidecar_daemon_manager.cjs:44-49 (readDiscoveryFile)
- frontend/src/main/sidecar_daemon_manager.cjs:133-146 (SidecarDaemonNodeClient.request)
- frontend/src/main/sidecar_daemon_manager.cjs:306-313 (reuseExistingDaemon)

The manager trusts base_url and token from a discovery file and immediately probes/reuses that endpoint. normalizeDiscovery only requires non-empty strings; it does not restrict scheme, host, or loopback address. The client then sends the x-windie-sidecar-token header to `${baseUrl}${pathname}` for health, tool execution, RPC, plugin registration, MCP registration, shutdown, and event connection. A malicious or stale discovery file can therefore make the Electron main process send privileged sidecar credentials and requests to a non-local endpoint.

recommendation:
Validate discovery base_url before using it. Restrict accepted discovery endpoints to loopback HTTP(S) hosts or the expected local daemon origin, reject unsupported protocols, and delete invalid discovery files. Consider binding token acceptance to the expected pid/host/port if available.

test analysis:
No tests are included for sidecar_daemon_manager.cjs discovery validation, and the feature metadata lists no linked tests.

suggested regression test:
Add tests for read/reuse discovery that reject http://example.com, file-like or malformed schemes, and non-loopback hosts, while accepting 127.0.0.1/localhost loopback daemon URLs.

minimum fix scope:
frontend/src/main/sidecar_daemon_manager.cjs discovery normalization/reuse path plus focused manager tests.

## medium: Stale hydration can mutate session bindings after connection invalidation

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-7d14198519-6d66_42bef172ab
category: concurrency
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/session (feat_library_7d14198519)

evidence:
- frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/session/conversationInferenceSessionRuntime.ts:119-123 (invalidateConversationInferenceSessionState)
- frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/session/conversationInferenceSessionRuntime.ts:150-166 (ensureConversationInferenceSessionHydrated)
- frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/session/conversationInferenceSessionRuntime.ts:188-196 (rehydrateConversationInferenceSession)

The module tracks connectionEpoch and clears sync/in-flight state on invalidation, which implies async hydration from an old connection should not affect the current session. However, ensureConversationInferenceSessionHydrated captures startingEpoch but performs setConversationWorkspaceBinding and rehydrateFromStore before checking whether the epoch is still current; only the final hydrated state marker is guarded. Similarly, rehydrateConversationInferenceSession sends rehydrateMessages before the epoch check. If invalidateConversationInferenceSessionState runs while a snapshot load or rehydrate is in progress, the stale async continuation can still write a workspace binding or rehydrate messages into the now-current backend session, even though it will not mark the local state hydrated.

recommendation:
Check startingEpoch against connectionEpoch immediately after each awaited operation and before any side effects, or pass a cancellable/session token through the continuity service so stale hydration cannot update workspace bindings or backend state after invalidation.

test analysis:
No tests are included for this feature, and the code has no visible regression coverage for invalidation occurring between snapshot load and backend rehydration.

suggested regression test:
Add a unit test that starts ensureConversationInferenceSessionHydrated with a deferred loadLocalConversationSnapshot promise, calls invalidateConversationInferenceSessionState before resolving it, then asserts setConversationWorkspaceBinding and rehydrateFromStore are not called for the stale epoch.

minimum fix scope:
frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/session/conversationInferenceSessionRuntime.ts

## medium: Stream usage can return stale data after a stream with no usage payload

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-ed619aedb2-ff00_4534a39bca
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/llm/providers/:base (feat_library_ed619aedb2)

evidence:
- backend/src/llm/providers/base.py:230-247 (LLMProvider.get_completion_stream)
- backend/src/llm/providers/base.py:249-259 (LLMProvider.clear_last_stream_usage)
- backend/src/llm/providers/base.py:299-317 (LLMProvider._record_stream_usage_from_chunk)

The provider stores stream usage on the instance, but get_completion_stream does not clear the previous values before starting a new stream. _record_stream_usage_from_chunk only updates _last_stream_usage when a later chunk contains usage, and get_last_stream_usage returns the stored value. If a previous stream captured usage and the next stream fails early or completes without provider usage metadata, callers can read the earlier request's token/cache usage as if it belonged to the latest stream.

recommendation:
Clear _last_stream_usage, _last_usage, and _last_stream_response_payload at the start of each streaming request, or make the usage payload request-scoped and returned through the stream/result path instead of provider instance state.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for this feature, and this requires a sequential two-request regression where the second stream lacks usage metadata.

suggested regression test:
Add a provider test with a fake _stream_internal that records usage on the first stream and emits no usage on the second, then assert get_last_stream_usage() is None after the second stream completes.

minimum fix scope:
Update get_completion_stream to reset stream-scoped state before iterating _stream_internal, plus add a focused provider base regression test.

repro:
Run two streaming requests through the same provider instance: first with a response that includes usage, then a second that emits no usage chunks or errors before a usage chunk. After consuming the second stream, call get_last_stream_usage(); it can still return the first stream's payload.

## medium: Streaming error path can emit partial content before the terminal error

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-38cf037009-b1be_1ee0c9819c
category: api-contract
confidence: medium
triage: contract-mismatch
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/agent/llm (feat_library_38cf037009)

evidence:
- backend/src/agent/llm/llm_stream_processor.py:138-151 (LLMStreamProcessor.get_response)
- backend/src/agent/llm/llm_stream_processor.py:202-205 (LLMStreamProcessor.get_response)
- tests/backend/test_llm_stream_processor.py:128-140 (_UnsupportedEventLLMClient)
- tests/backend/test_llm_stream_processor.py:469-484 (test_rejects_unsupported_stream_event_types)

If a provider stream yields one or more valid ChunkEvent values and then yields an unsupported event, the processor will already have emitted those chunks to the frontend, then catch the TypeError and emit ErrorEvent before re-raising. The included unsupported-event test only covers the first-event failure case, so it verifies an ErrorEvent is produced but does not cover the mixed partial-success/late-failure contract. Consumers can observe partial assistant text followed by an error without a FullResponseEvent or TokenCountEvent, which is an inconsistent terminal stream shape and can leave UI/history code with transient content that should not be committed.

recommendation:
Define the terminal contract for late stream failures. If partial chunks must be shown, emit an explicit failure terminal event/metadata that downstream code can use to discard or mark the partial assistant text. If partial text must not escape on invalid provider events, validate provider events before yielding user-visible chunks or buffer until the stream is known valid.

test analysis:
The existing unsupported-event regression starts with the unsupported object, so no partial ChunkEvent is emitted before the error path is exercised.

suggested regression test:
Add a fake stream client that yields a ChunkEvent followed by an unsupported object, then assert the intended contract: either partial chunks are absent, or the ErrorEvent carries enough terminal/failure metadata and no history-commit event follows.

minimum fix scope:
LLMStreamProcessor.get_response stream loop and one focused test in tests/backend/test_llm_stream_processor.py

repro:
Use an LLM client whose get_completion_stream yields ChunkEvent(content='partial') and then {'event':'unsupported'}. Iterating get_response will yield the chunk, then yield ErrorEvent from the generic exception handler, and then raise TypeError, with no FullResponseEvent or TokenCountEvent.

## medium: Tail budget trimming can leave invalid orphan tool messages in retained history

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-b5dbc2a239-2b8d_efcecbcd18
category: api-contract
confidence: medium
triage: contract-mismatch
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/agent/compaction (feat_library_b5dbc2a239)

evidence:
- backend/src/agent/compaction/engine.py:281-286
- backend/src/agent/compaction/engine.py:311-320
- backend/src/agent/compaction/engine.py:175-187

The initial split keeps recent history starting at a USER_QUERY, but _fit_tail_to_target_budget then repeatedly removes the first retained tail message one at a time until the tail fits. That trimming is role-agnostic, so it can remove the user message or assistant tool-call message while retaining a following tool result. The final replacement history is then summary + remaining tail, which may contain tool messages without their required preceding assistant tool_calls. That can violate downstream LLM message contracts or make replayed tool context incoherent.

recommendation:
Trim retained tail at conversation-turn/tool-exchange boundaries instead of individual messages. At minimum, when dropping from the tail, drop a whole user turn including assistant tool-call and tool-result sequence, and validate that the replacement history has no orphan tool messages before replacing session history.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included, and there is no evidence of a test that constructs oversized recent tool-use history and validates post-compaction message ordering.

suggested regression test:
Create a history with a recent user query, assistant tool_calls, and tool result where the retained tail exceeds target_tokens; after compaction, assert the replacement history either preserves the complete tool-call/result sequence or drops the whole sequence without leaving a leading tool result.

minimum fix scope:
CompactionEngine._fit_tail_to_target_budget plus focused validation/tests for retained tail role ordering.

## medium: Tests codify duplicate tool-call IDs as acceptable

id: fnd_sig-feat-test-suite-de689544bf-d_492f7d52e3
category: api-contract
confidence: medium
triage: contract-mismatch
status: open
feature: Python test suite tests/backend#6 (feat_test-suite_de689544bf)

evidence:
- tests/backend/test_llm_provider_utils.py:416-439 (test_extract_completion_response_keeps_same_id_when_tool_names_differ)

The test suite asserts that two tool calls with the same id but different names should be preserved unchanged. Tool-call IDs are the join key used by later tool response messages, so preserving duplicate IDs makes the assistant-to-tool-result association ambiguous and can route or validate the wrong result against a call. This is especially risky because nearby tests also enforce dropping orphan tool messages by matching tool_call_id, so the contract relies on IDs being usable as unique references.

recommendation:
Change the intended contract to reject duplicate tool-call IDs in a single assistant message, or normalize them into unique IDs with an explicit mapping before any tool responses are generated. Update this test to cover that behavior instead of preserving duplicates.

test analysis:
The current test covers the opposite behavior: it locks in duplicate-ID preservation as expected, so a uniqueness guard would fail the suite.

suggested regression test:
Replace this case with a test that passes two raw tool calls sharing id=call_1 and asserts normalize/extract raises LLMAPIError, or asserts deterministic unique IDs are emitted and later tool responses can be matched unambiguously.

minimum fix scope:
Update response/tool-call normalization and the provider utility test that currently expects duplicate ID preservation.

## medium: Tool outputs with null payloads are dropped as malformed

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-82ea571cf9-4cac_761965b19b

evidence:
- backend/src/api/processing/formatters/tool_output.py:18-31

The formatter treats output is None as a missing required field and returns None, so a successful tool execution whose legitimate result is JSON null/Python None is never emitted to clients. That can leave the UI or remote-tool matcher without the terminal TOOL_OUTPUT event even though tool_name and success are present. The formatter already distinguishes missing parameters in tool_call by checking key presence semantics less precisely, but here it conflates absence with a valid null value for the output field.

recommendation:
Validate required field presence with key membership, not value nullness, for output. For example, require "output" in event_dict while allowing event_dict["output"] to be None if the tool result is null.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for this feature, and the excerpt has no regression coverage showing null tool results remain streamable.

suggested regression test:
Add a formatter test that passes a ToolOutputEvent/dict with tool_name set, success true, and output None, and asserts a TOOL_OUTPUT payload is returned with output null.

minimum fix scope:
Change ToolOutputEventFormatter required-field validation for output to check field presence rather than output is None, then add focused formatter coverage.

## medium: Tool schema events serialize raw ToolSchema objects

minimum fix scope:

repro:
Instantiate ToolSchemasEvent with a non-dict ToolSchema object and pass event.to_dict() to a JSON encoder; the returned tool_schemas list will still contain the original object unless ToolSchema itself is already a dict-like type.

## medium: Tool-loop response extraction can return a pre-tool response instead of the final answer

- backend/src/sdk/agents/response_extractor.py:76-95 (extract_response)

FullResponseEvent is described in-code as the LLM response for the current iteration, but the extractor only accepts the first non-empty full response and then refuses to overwrite it. backend/src/sdk/agents/response_extractor.py plus a focused backend SDK test.

## medium: Turn refs are stored unnormalized when inferred from messages

id: fnd_sig-feat-service-37c269c6cc-4d39_5d19f44cde
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/stores (feat_service_37c269c6cc)

evidence:
- frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/stores/chatStore.ts:293-317 (registerTurnConversationRef / resolveConversationRefForTurn)
- frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/stores/chatStore.ts:331-336 (addMessage)
- frontend/src/renderer/features/chat/stores/chatStore.ts:360-365 (updateMessage)

The explicit registration and lookup path trims turn refs before storing and resolving them, but the implicit registration done by addMessage and updateMessage writes message.turnRef / updates.turnRef directly. If an upstream event supplies a turnRef with leading or trailing whitespace, addMessage stores the untrimmed key while resolveConversationRefForTurn trims the lookup key, so the conversation cannot be resolved for that turn. updateMessage also treats whitespace-only turn refs as valid because it checks length without trimming. This can misroute or drop later turn-scoped updates in multi-conversation chat state.

recommendation:
Normalize turn refs in addMessage and updateMessage with the same trim logic used by registerTurnConversationRef, and ignore empty normalized turn refs.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for this source group, and the package manifest only exposes the Jest commands.

suggested regression test:
Add a store unit test that adds and updates messages with padded and whitespace-only turnRef values, then verifies resolveConversationRefForTurn returns the conversation only for normalized non-empty refs.

minimum fix scope:
Change the implicit turn-ref registration branches in addMessage and updateMessage to compute a normalizedTurnRef before updating turnConversationRefs.

repro:
Call addMessage({ id: 'm1', text: 'x', sender: 'assistant', turnRef: ' turn-1 ' }, 'conv-a'), then resolveConversationRefForTurn(' turn-1 '); the resolver trims to 'turn-1' but the map key was stored as ' turn-1 ', so it returns null.

## medium: Unidentified tool outputs can be paired with unrelated identified calls during replay

Only use the positional fallback when both sides are idless, or when a documented legacy replay mode explicitly permits positional matching. If pending calls have correlation ids and the output does not, leave the output unmatched.

No linked tests were included for replay filtering or mismatched correlation-id behavior.

suggested regression test:
Add a test where messages contain two correlated tool-call entries followed by an idless tool-output, and assert buildReplayContextMessages drops the idless output instead of preserving it with the first call.

minimum fix scope:
Update findMatchingPendingToolCallIndex matching rules and add focused replay-correlation tests.

Add an explicit auth/session dependency to the SDK router or this route, or document and test the mount-level dependency that protects it. Keep the authorization check close enough to the route that future router reuse cannot accidentally expose the endpoint.

test analysis:

suggested regression test:
Add a FastAPI route test for POST /api/sdk/vision/locate that verifies requests without the required auth/session context receive 401/403, plus an authorized request still reaches the vision service path with mocked dependencies.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/api/routes/sdk/router.py and the corresponding SDK route tests

## medium: Vision logs can persist sensitive screenshot-derived text

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-9352222a5b-b3f6_a1ae5cf38a
category: security
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/services/vision (feat_library_9352222a5b)

evidence:

- backend/src/services/vision/providers/ui_venus.py:236-246
- backend/src/services/vision/providers/internvl_runtime_helpers.py:97-98

This feature processes screenshots/images that may contain private UI text. InternVL logs up to 1000 characters of the raw model response at warning level and logs the full output when coordinate parsing fails; Venus does the same. The shared chat helper also logs the full chat response. For image-question APIs, responses may contain OCR-like descriptions or user-visible data from the screenshot, so these logs can persist sensitive screen contents in backend logs. The code already hashes/sanitizes the user instruction for one log path, which suggests logs are expected to avoid raw user/image-derived content, but the model responses bypass that protection.

recommendation:
Do not log raw vision model responses by default. Log only bounded metadata such as response length, a stable hash, parse status, and perhaps a very short redacted preview behind an explicit debug flag. Also avoid logging full unparsed output on parse failures.

test analysis:
tests/backend/test_vision_service.py only covers VisionService initialization, provider selection, and unload state; it does not exercise provider inference logging or assert redaction behavior.

suggested regression test:
Add focused provider/helper tests with a fake model response containing a sentinel secret and assert normal/error logs do not include the sentinel while still recording parse success/failure metadata.

minimum fix scope:
Update InternVL, Venus, and shared runtime helper logging for raw model responses and parse failures; add redaction-focused tests around those log paths.

## medium: Visual anchor height IPC never applies the chat window frame resize path

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-f6c5c85f58-fdfd_04ba1d587e
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/main/:overlay (feat_library_f6c5c85f58)

evidence:
- frontend/src/main/overlay_phase_ipc_runtime.cjs:45-55 (initializeOverlayPhaseHandlersRuntime)
- frontend/src/main/overlay_chatbox_visual_anchor_handler.cjs:23-30 (handleSetChatboxVisualAnchorHeight)
- frontend/src/main/overlay_window_helpers_runtime.cjs:235-283 (setChatWindowBoundsForVisualAnchorHeight)

The IPC runtime passes setChatWindowBoundsForVisualAnchorHeight and resizeChatWindowForVisualAnchorHeight into the visual-anchor handler, and the helper has a dedicated path that preserves the chat window bottom edge while changing the frame height. The handler never consumes either dependency; it only records the anchor height and repositions the response/context windows. That means a renderer update to the visual anchor height can leave the actual BrowserWindow frame at its old height, so the anchored overlay math changes without the chat surface bounds being updated to match. This is especially risky for the minimal-pill/awaiting layout where the response window is positioned off the visual anchor rather than the full chat frame.

recommendation:
Have handleSetChatboxVisualAnchorHeight invoke the bottom-preserving bounds update, likely setChatWindowBoundsForVisualAnchorHeight(nextHeight) as the primary path, and only then reposition dependent windows. Remove the unused resize dependency if it is not part of the intended contract, or use it explicitly as a fallback with tests documenting the precedence.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for this feature, and the dead dependency passed through the IPC layer would only be caught by a focused handler/helper test that asserts the chat window bounds are updated when the visual anchor height changes.

suggested regression test:
Add a frontend main-process unit test for set-chatbox-visual-anchor-height/handleSetChatboxVisualAnchorHeight using stub windows. Assert that changing the anchor height calls the bottom-preserving chat-window bounds updater before response/context repositioning, and that unchanged/invalid heights do not reposition unnecessarily.

minimum fix scope:
frontend/src/main/overlay_chatbox_visual_anchor_handler.cjs plus a focused frontend test for the handler or IPC runtime wiring.

## medium: VM worker can dispatch duplicate conversation runs before backend events arrive

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-69aa448df6-e212_dde43d33d4
category: concurrency
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/main#2 (feat_library_69aa448df6)

evidence:
- frontend/src/main/vm_worker_runtime.cjs:201-203 (dispatchAssignedRun)
- frontend/src/main/vm_worker_runtime.cjs:211-229 (dispatchAssignedRun)
- frontend/src/main/vm_worker_runtime.cjs:296-319 (heartbeatTick)

dispatchAssignedRun only checks whether the run_id is already active, and it records the run/conversation mapping only after sendAutomatedQuery succeeds. During that gap the next heartbeat still reports ready when no active mapping exists and can accept another assigned_run for the same conversation_ref or a different run targeting the same conversation. This allows overlapping automated queries for the same conversation and ambiguous run event routing once mappings are later set.

recommendation:
Reserve the run/conversation mapping before awaiting sendAutomatedQuery, and clear it on dispatch failure. Also reject assignments when either the run_id or conversation_ref is already active/pending.

test analysis:
No VM worker runtime tests are included, and there is no test evidence covering slow dispatch or duplicate heartbeat assignment races.

suggested regression test:
Add a unit test with a deferred sendAutomatedQuery and two assigned_run heartbeat responses for the same conversation_ref, asserting only one query dispatch occurs and the worker status becomes running while dispatch is pending.

minimum fix scope:
frontend/src/main/vm_worker_runtime.cjs dispatch bookkeeping plus focused Jest coverage.

repro:
Have the heartbeat API return an assigned_run while sendAutomatedQuery is slow, then return another assigned_run with a different run_id but the same conversation_ref before the first call stores the mapping. Both automated queries can be dispatched.

## medium: Wakeword detections are handled even when detection is disabled

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-0605e3afd8-d2d2_7cc2ffff4d
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/features/voice (feat_library_0605e3afd8)

evidence:
- frontend/src/renderer/features/voice/hooks/useWakewordBridgeEvents.ts:34-69 (useWakewordBridgeEvents)
- frontend/src/renderer/features/voice/hooks/useWakewordDetection.ts:282-292 (useWakewordBridgeEvents call)
- frontend/src/renderer/features/voice/hooks/useWakewordDetection.ts:294-321 (enabled effect)

The bridge event subscription remains active regardless of enabled, and the WAKEWORD_DETECTED handler never checks enabled before applying cooldown, sending WAKEWORD_DISABLE, and invoking onWakewordDetectedRef. The disabled branch asks the main process to disable wakeword capture, but any late or queued detection event after disabled can still fire the user callback and trigger the voice flow.

recommendation:
Ignore detection events when enabled is false, or unsubscribe/subscribe conditionally so disabled mode cannot invoke the wakeword callback. Keep status events if needed, but gate detection side effects on the current enabled state via a latest ref.

test analysis:
No tests are included for bridge event behavior, late IPC delivery, or disabled-state callback suppression.

suggested regression test:
Render the hook with enabled false, emit WAKEWORD_DETECTED through a mocked IpcBridge, and assert WAKEWORD_DISABLE and onWakewordDetected are not called.

minimum fix scope:
useWakewordBridgeEvents detection handler gating, with a focused hook test around disabled and late-event behavior.

repro:
Enable wakeword detection, then disable it and deliver a queued WAKEWORD_DETECTED IPC event after the disabled branch runs. The handler still invokes onWakewordDetectedRef if confidence passes threshold and cooldown has elapsed.

## medium: Worker assignment can permanently drop runs reserved for another worker

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-ef7a926afe-5f60_19237a6369
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/services/vm_run_control_support (feat_library_ef7a926afe)

evidence:
- backend/src/services/vm_run_control_support/vm_run_control_assignment.py:35-47 (assign_next_run_to_worker)
- tests/backend/test_vm_run_control_assignment.py:23-62 (test_assign_next_run_to_worker_assigns_eligible_run)

assign_next_run_to_worker destructively pops run IDs from workspace_queue before checking whether the queued run is assigned to a different worker. When assigned_worker.worker_id is neither None nor the current worker_id, the function continues without requeueing that run ID. In a workspace-level queue, this can strand a valid run that is reserved for another worker: the current worker removes it from the queue, does not assign it, and the intended worker will no longer see it through the queue. The existing assignment test only covers an unassigned eligible run and asserts the queue is emptied after success; it does not cover the different-worker branch.

recommendation:
Preserve queue membership for runs that are still eligible but owned by another worker, for example by scanning without destructive removal, rotating skipped reserved runs back onto the queue, or maintaining per-worker queues. Only discard queue entries for missing runs or terminal/non-queued runs where removal is intentional.

test analysis:
The assignment tests cover not-ready workers and a successful assignment for an unassigned run, but they never enqueue a run with worker.worker_id set to a different worker and assert that it remains dispatchable.

suggested regression test:
Add a test with queue ['run-1'], run status 'queued', and worker {'worker_id': 'worker-b'}; call assign_next_run_to_worker for 'worker-a' and assert it returns None while preserving 'run-1' for worker-b or otherwise keeping the run reachable by the intended worker.

minimum fix scope:
Update assign_next_run_to_worker queue handling for the different-assigned-worker branch and add a focused assignment regression test.

## low: AVX2 probe reads 32 bytes from argv storage

id: fnd_sig-feat-cli-command-1788578c63-_efa889531c
category: bug
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: false-positive
feature: C binary cpu_avx2 (feat_cli-command_1788578c63)

evidence:
- frontend/python-runtime/lib/python3.11/site-packages/numpy/distutils/checks/cpu_avx2.c:16-19 (main)

The program treats argv[argc-1] as a 32-byte __m256i input and performs an unbounded 256-bit load from it. When executed, argv[argc-1] can be argv[0] or a short argument string, so the load can read past the argument object's bounds and invoke undefined behavior or crash under sanitizers/hardened runtimes. The file appears intended as an instruction availability check, so it can exercise AVX2 without depending on process argument storage.

recommendation:
Use a fixed local 32-byte buffer with known contents, then load from that buffer before calling the AVX2 intrinsic.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for this feature, and nothing in the excerpt exercises execution with short or absent user arguments.

suggested regression test:
Compile and run the probe under AddressSanitizer with no extra arguments and with a one-byte argument; it should exit without out-of-bounds reads.

minimum fix scope:
Change main to load from a local 32-byte array instead of argv[argc-1].

## low: Closing a conversation runtime leaves event listeners retained

SdkConversationRuntime owns both snapshot listeners and event listeners, but close() only clears snapshot listeners. minimum fix scope:
Update SdkConversationRuntime.close() to clear eventListeners and add focused runtime disposal coverage.

repro:
Create a chat session, register an onEvent listener that closes over a large object, call chat.close(), then inspect the runtime behavior by applying or receiving a later event; the event listener set was not cleared by close().

## low: Initialization failure messages report the last completed phase, not the failing phase

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-eb603057ae-61bc_eb50cf627d
category: bug
confidence: high
triage: confirmed-bug
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/core/bootstrap (feat_library_eb603057ae)

- backend/src/core/bootstrap/coordinator.py:109-120 (InitializationCoordinator.initialize)
- backend/src/core/bootstrap/coordinator.py:129-140 (InitializationCoordinator.initialize)

The phase list is appended only after each phase succeeds, but the exception handler formats the error using self._initialized_phases[-1]. If container initialization fails, the error says it failed at 'configuration'; if service initialization fails, it says 'container'; and if configuration fails, it says 'unknown'. This makes startup failures misleading and can send operators toward the wrong subsystem.

recommendation:
Track the current phase before entering each phase, and use that current phase in the error message and log. Keep _initialized_phases only for rollback of completed phases.

repro:

## low: Cache cannot reliably cache None results

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-2fdb111c39-5b4c_5b9c2f7f5e
category: api-contract
confidence: medium
triage: contract-mismatch
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/core/infrastructure (feat_library_2fdb111c39)

evidence:
- backend/src/core/infrastructure/cache_store.py:51-75 (Cache.get)
- backend/src/core/infrastructure/cache_store.py:86-106 (Cache.set)
- backend/src/core/infrastructure/cache_store.py:171-189 (Cache.get_or_compute)
- backend/src/core/infrastructure/cache_store.py:208-226 (Cache.get_or_compute_async)

Cache.set accepts Any values, but Cache.get returns None both for misses and for stored None values. The get_or_compute methods treat value is not None as the only cache-hit condition, so a compute function that legitimately returns None is recomputed on every call even though the cache contains an unexpired entry. This breaks the generic cache contract for a valid Python value and can create repeated expensive calls or repeated side effects.

recommendation:
Separate miss detection from value retrieval with a sentinel or internal lookup method that returns entry presence independently of entry.value. Preserve public get behavior only if callers depend on None-as-miss, but make get_or_compute use the unambiguous internal path.

test analysis:
The included CacheManager tests only cache string values and assert misses return None; they do not cover storing None or compute functions that return None.

suggested regression test:
Add sync and async get_or_compute tests where compute_func returns None and assert the function is invoked once while subsequent calls return None from the cached entry.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/core/infrastructure/cache_store.py and focused cache_store tests.

repro:
Call cache.get_or_compute('k', lambda: None) twice. The second call enters the miss path again because get('k') returns None for the cached value.

The cleanup path cancels an in-flight websocket.receive task but does not await it, while the provider stream task is both cancelled and awaited. This is especially likely when the provider stream ends first while the receive task is still blocked.

recommendation:
After cancelling receive_task, await it under contextlib.suppress(asyncio.CancelledError, Exception) or otherwise consume its terminal state consistently with provider_stream_task cleanup.

No linked tests are included for this feature, and the existing code path only exposes the issue during teardown timing where provider_stream_task completes before websocket.receive.

Add an async websocket route teardown test with a fake provider stream that completes while receive is pending, then assert cleanup awaits/cancels both background tasks without pending-task warnings.

minimum fix scope:
Update the finally block in backend/src/api/routes/transcription/router.py to await receive_task after cancellation.

## low: Failed flush requeues older messages behind newly enqueued messages

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-1a017caec3-427a_67ff682ca4
category: concurrency
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/transcript/pending (feat_library_1a017caec3)

- frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/transcript/pending/transcriptPendingFlush.ts:28-39
- frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/transcript/pending/pendingTranscriptMessages.ts:88-105

flushPendingMessages drains a snapshot of pending messages, then awaits storeTranscriptEntry per entry. If new retry messages are enqueued while the await is pending and a later store fails, requeuePending appends the unflushed older snapshot entries to the tail of the live queue. A later flush can then persist newer messages before older failed messages, corrupting transcript ordering under an async failure interleaving.

recommendation:
Requeue failed snapshot entries ahead of any messages that arrived during the flush, or serialize flush/enqueue ordering with an in-flight buffer so older drained entries retain priority after retry.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included, and this requires a storeTranscriptEntry mock that yields to allow enqueueing during an in-flight flush before throwing.

suggested regression test:
Add a test that starts a flush with two pending user messages, lets the second store call yield, enqueues a third message before the second call rejects, then verifies the next flush persists the second message before the third.

minimum fix scope:
Adjust queue reinsert semantics or pending flush synchronization for the pending transcript queues, with a focused async ordering regression test.

Several exception constructors mirror caller-supplied context into attributes, but metadata inclusion is gated with bool(field_value) or any(fields.values()). Update the metadata helper predicates in base.py and any affected trust-boundary helper logic, then add regression tests for falsy context values.

repro:
Instantiate ParseTimeoutError('timeout', timeout_seconds=0) or InputSizeLimitError('limit', actual_size=0, max_size=0) and inspect error.metadata; the provided size/timeout fields are absent while the attributes are set.

## low: Feature probe can dereference past argv when run without arguments

id: fnd_sig-feat-cli-command-0a38be6a56-_575b396d48
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: false-positive
feature: C binary cpu_avx512_icl (feat_cli-command_0a38be6a56)

evidence:
- frontend/python-runtime/lib/python3.11/site-packages/numpy/distutils/checks/cpu_avx512_icl.c:16-18 (main)

main reads argv[argc-1] and passes it to _mm512_loadu_si512. If the binary is executed with argc == 0 in a nonstandard process launch, this indexes before argv. More commonly with argc == 1 it reads from argv[0], which is a short NUL-terminated string and the 64-byte unaligned vector load can read beyond the argument string object. As a compile/run feature check this may usually be invoked by build tooling rather than end users, so severity is low, but the top-level CLI entrypoint is not robust to its declared user-input/process-exec boundary.

recommendation:
Avoid loading from argv storage. Use a local aligned or unaligned 64-byte buffer initialized in the function, or guard argc and input length before loading from argv.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for this feature probe or for direct execution behavior.

suggested regression test:
Add a build/probe test that compiles and executes cpu_avx512_icl with no extra CLI arguments under the expected probe harness and verifies it exits without invalid memory access on supported hardware or an emulator environment.

minimum fix scope:
Change main to load from a local 64-byte buffer instead of argv, preserving the intrinsic calls used to validate compiler support.

- backend/src/agent/tools/preparation/validation.py:215-230 (sanitize_and_validate_resolved_tool_call)
- backend/src/agent/tools/preparation/validation.py:244-253 (_strip_grounding_only_fields)

sanitize_and_validate_resolved_tool_call calls _strip_grounding_only_fields before validating executor arguments. _strip_grounding_only_fields unconditionally calls resolved_call.parameters.pop for mouse_control and scroll_control, while the later model validation explicitly tolerates missing parameters via resolved_call.parameters or {}. A malformed resolved call with parameters=None will therefore raise AttributeError during sanitization instead of returning the intended validation error message.

recommendation:
Normalize resolved_call.parameters to an empty dict before stripping, or make _strip_grounding_only_fields return early when parameters is not a mutable dict. Then let model validation produce the controlled preparation error.

test analysis:
No linked tests were provided for malformed resolved calls, and the current order means the validation error path is bypassed before Pydantic can report missing required fields.

suggested regression test:
Add a unit test for sanitize_and_validate_resolved_tool_call on a mouse_control ResolvedToolCall with parameters=None and enabled=True, asserting a validation error string is returned and no AttributeError is raised.

minimum fix scope:
Harden _strip_grounding_only_fields against None/non-dict parameters and add one validation regression test.

## low: Ingress reports unhandled events as accepted even when dispatch rejects them

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-cd45c89b51-c8c9_8ac4416599
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/app/runtime#1 (feat_library_cd45c89b51)

- frontend/src/renderer/app/runtime/desktopChatStreamIngressRuntime.ts:145-146 (ingestBackendEvent)

handleBackendStreamIngress normalizes a backend event to a ConversationEvent, then dispatches it through dispatchConversationEvent, whose boolean result is ignored. ingestBackendEvent returns true after dispatchEvent regardless of whether the normalized event was null or dispatchConversationEvent returned false. Callers and traces therefore see the event as accepted even when it was not actually dispatched to the SDK conversation stream, masking dropped or unsupported backend events.

recommendation:
Propagate the dispatchConversationEvent boolean through ingestBackendEvent, or return false when normalization produces null and no conversation event is dispatched. Keep any side-effect-only event types explicit if they are intentionally accepted without dispatch.

test analysis:
No linked tests exercise handleBackendStreamIngress with an unsupported backend event or a dispatchConversationEvent implementation that returns false.

suggested regression test:
Add a test where normalizeBackendIngressEvent returns null or dispatchConversationEvent returns false, then assert handleBackendStreamIngress returns false and logs the rejected state.

minimum fix scope:
frontend/src/renderer/app/runtime/desktopChatStreamIngressRuntime.ts and a focused ingress-runtime test.

## low: Invalid send channels fail silently while invoke channels report an error

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-0a20863e2d-24d1_e6b4c06bae
category: api-contract
confidence: medium
triage: contract-mismatch
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src (feat_library_0a20863e2d)

evidence:
- frontend/src/preload.js:32-42

`send` drops invalid channels without throwing, returning a rejected promise, or otherwise signaling failure, while `invoke` rejects invalid channels with a clear error. Renderer callers can therefore believe a fire-and-forget command was accepted even when it was blocked by the preload whitelist, which makes IPC wiring mistakes hard to detect and can produce stuck UI flows with no observable failure path.

recommendation:
Make invalid `send` channels fail explicitly, for example by throwing an `Error` or returning a boolean/status that callers can check, and keep the behavior documented consistently with `invoke`.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for invalid IPC channel handling, and the shown package scripts only indicate general Jest coverage rather than a specific preload contract test.

suggested regression test:
Add a preload IPC test that calls `window.ipc.send` with an invalid channel and asserts a deterministic failure signal, plus a valid-channel case that still delegates to `ipcRenderer.send`.

minimum fix scope:
Update `frontend/src/preload.js` invalid-channel handling for `send` and add focused tests for valid and invalid send paths.

## low: Malformed audio chunks are skipped with synchronous recursion

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-71f2fc3b5e-601a_6b39cd40b8
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/audio (feat_library_71f2fc3b5e)

evidence:
- frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/audio/PlayerService.ts:76-115 (PlayerService.playNext)

When decoding or buffer creation fails, the catch handler calls playNext() directly. If the queue contains many malformed chunks, each failure recurses synchronously before the stack unwinds because atob/createBuffer errors happen before source.start schedules any async boundary. A burst of bad chunks can therefore overflow the call stack and leave playback state inconsistent instead of draining safely.

recommendation:
Schedule the next skip on a microtask or loop through failed chunks iteratively, and ensure isPlaying/activeSource are reset if draining aborts unexpectedly.

test analysis:
No tests are included for PlayerService error-drain behavior, and the package test scripts only show a Jest harness without linked coverage for this service.

suggested regression test:
Add a Jest test that mocks window.atob to throw for many queued chunks and verifies enqueueAudio drains without throwing RangeError/stack overflow and ends with getIsPlaying() false.

minimum fix scope:
Change the catch path in PlayerService.playNext so failed chunks advance asynchronously or via an iterative drain, then add focused PlayerService tests.

repro:
Enqueue a large number of chunks with invalid base64 or invalid PCM byte length while playback is idle; each failed chunk enters the catch path and calls playNext() synchronously until the stack limit is hit.

## low: Malformed odd-length PCM16 buffers can raise uncaught resampling errors

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-9a79195fcc-849b_49310a05c1
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/api/services/transcription (feat_library_9a79195fcc)

evidence:
- backend/src/api/services/transcription/audio_frames.py:42-60 (resample_pcm16_mono)
- backend/src/api/services/transcription/openai_realtime.py:67-80 (OpenAIRealtimeTranscriptionSession.handle_audio_chunk)
- backend/src/api/services/transcription/protocol.py:20-21 (TranscriptionProviderSession.handle_audio_chunk)

The protocol says chunks are PCM16 mono, but resample_pcm16_mono passes arbitrary bytes directly to np.frombuffer(dtype=np.int16). If a malformed or truncated audio chunk has an odd byte length and needs resampling, NumPy raises ValueError because the buffer size is not a multiple of int16. handle_audio_chunk does not catch or normalize this, so one malformed frame can fail the provider task instead of being rejected predictably.

recommendation:
Validate that PCM16 input length is even before np.frombuffer. Either raise a clear ValueError at the frame boundary, drop the trailing byte with logging, or reject the frame without tearing down the streaming session.

test analysis:
No linked audio-frame tests were included, and the helper lacks an explicit edge-case guard for malformed PCM16 byte lengths.

suggested regression test:
Add an audio_frames unit test for odd-length PCM16 input during resampling and assert the chosen behavior is deterministic and does not surface an unhandled NumPy exception.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/api/services/transcription/audio_frames.py input validation plus a focused helper test.

repro:
Call resample_pcm16_mono(b'\x00', 16000, 24000) or OpenAIRealtimeTranscriptionSession.handle_audio_chunk with a one-byte buffer and a non-24000 sample rate.

## low: Memory retrieval preference helpers can throw on storage access failures

- frontend/src/renderer/utils/memoryRetrievalPreference.js:35-44

The memory retrieval preference helpers call getItem and setItem on the resolved storage object without the try/catch protection used by configStorage. Browser storage operations can throw, for example when storage is unavailable, disabled, or quota/security policies reject access. That turns a non-critical preference read or write into a renderer exception instead of falling back to the default enabled value or returning the normalized setting.

recommendation:
Wrap storage getItem/setItem calls in try/catch. Reads should return the safe default true when storage fails, and writes should return the normalized preference while optionally logging a warning.

test analysis:
No tests are included for this feature, and there is no evidence of a fake storage object that throws on getItem or setItem.

suggested regression test:
Add unit tests using a storage stub whose getItem and setItem throw, asserting getMemoryRetrievalInjectionEnabled returns true and setMemoryRetrievalInjectionEnabled returns the normalized boolean without throwing.

minimum fix scope:
Update memoryRetrievalPreference.js to handle storage exceptions for both read and write paths.

## low: One-time IPC listeners cannot be cleaned up before they fire

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-0a20863e2d-70c2_a6771b83ec
category: performance
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src (feat_library_0a20863e2d)

evidence:
- frontend/src/preload.js:45-59

The persistent listener API returns a cleanup function that removes the exact subscription, but the one-time listener API registers an anonymous wrapper and returns nothing. If renderer code installs a one-time listener for a channel and the component/request is cancelled or unmounted before the main process emits the event, that listener remains registered until a future event eventually fires. That can leak callbacks and can also deliver a stale event into a renderer flow that no longer owns the request.

recommendation:
Mirror the `on` API shape for `once`: create a named subscription wrapper, register it with `ipcRenderer.once`, and return a cleanup function that calls `ipcRenderer.removeListener(channel, subscription)`.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for the preload IPC listener lifecycle, and the excerpt has no coverage demonstrating cancellation/unmount behavior for one-time listeners.

suggested regression test:
Add a preload IPC test with a mocked `ipcRenderer` that calls `window.ipc.once`, invokes the returned cleanup before any event is emitted, and asserts `removeListener` is called with the same channel and wrapper.

minimum fix scope:
Change only `frontend/src/preload.js` listener registration for `once` and add a focused preload IPC lifecycle test.

## low: Repeated override refreshes stack provider overrides without cleanup

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-41e7c46fe2-c00c_dee3f653c8
category: performance
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/core/container/:api (feat_library_41e7c46fe2)

- backend/src/core/container/api_runtime.py:43-53 (ApiRuntimeBinder._sync_api_container_overrides)

Each refresh call applies new provider overrides to config, config_service, model_service, and session_manager. dependency_injector provider overrides are stacked unless reset, so repeated refresh_overrides calls can leave an unbounded chain of stale Object providers attached to each dependency provider. recommendation:
Before applying replacement overrides, reset the existing overrides for these dependency providers or replace the ApiContainer wholesale. If stacking is intentional, document the lifecycle and expose a cleanup path.

minimum fix scope:
ApiRuntimeBinder._sync_api_container_overrides and a focused lifecycle regression test.

feature: FastAPI route POST /api/artifacts/ (feat_route_486a6ec0f4)
