- backend/src/api/routes/artifacts/router.py:36-43 (upload_artifact)

The upload response constructs the artifact URL directly from request.base_url. In common ASGI deployments, that value can depend on Host or forwarded headers unless separately constrained by trusted-host/proxy middleware. A malicious or misconfigured inbound host can therefore cause the API to return an attacker-chosen absolute artifact URL, which is risky if clients persist, display, or later fetch the returned URL as authoritative.

recommendation:
Build artifact URLs from a configured public base URL, or return a relative path such as /api/artifacts/{artifact_id}. If request-derived hosts are required, ensure trusted host/proxy middleware is enforced and add a test for the expected base URL behavior.

test analysis:
No linked tests were included for response URL construction, and the handler has no local guard showing the request host has been validated.

suggested regression test:
Add a route test that sends a forged Host header and asserts the returned url uses the configured public base URL or remains relative.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/api/routes/artifacts/router.py and configuration/test coverage for artifact URL generation.

repro:
POST /api/artifacts/ with a forged Host header in a deployment that does not enforce trusted hosts; the response url field will use that host.

## low: Schema normalizer cannot accept the top-level function schema shape it appears to normalize

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-f05dc711c2-6392_d1a93dc336
category: api-contract
confidence: medium
triage: contract-mismatch
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/transcript#2 (feat_library_f05dc711c2)

evidence:
- frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/transcript/toolSchemaShape.ts:16-24 (normalizeFunctionToolSchema)
- frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/transcript/toolSchemaShape.ts:46-60 (isSupportedToolSchema)
- frontend/src/renderer/infrastructure/transcript/toolSchemaShape.ts:62-71 (normalizeToolSchema)

normalizeFunctionToolSchema has an explicit branch for schemas with top-level name and parameters, but normalizeToolSchema calls isSupportedToolSchema first, and that guard rejects every object without a string type before checking the function shape. As a result, a schema shaped as {name, parameters} can never reach the normalization branch that would wrap it as {type: 'function', function: ...}. If transparency payloads can contain that legacy/top-level function shape, the entire toolSchemas list is dropped by normalizeToolSchemaList rather than normalized.

recommendation:
Either remove the unreachable top-level normalization branch if the contract requires type: 'function', or update isSupportedToolSchema/normalizeToolSchema to allow top-level {name, parameters} and normalize it consistently.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for tool schema normalization, and the unreachable branch would only be caught by a test using a top-level {name, parameters} schema without type.

suggested regression test:
Add a normalizeToolSchemaList test with a top-level {name: 'x', parameters: {...}} item and assert it either normalizes to a function schema or is intentionally rejected after removing the dead branch.

minimum fix scope:
Align isSupportedToolSchema with normalizeFunctionToolSchema, plus a focused unit test for the accepted/rejected schema shapes.

## low: Semantic health can report ready without validating semantic service configuration

id: fnd_sig-feat-route-14b7feb076-c1384d_aadfb3fb2e
category: api-contract
confidence: medium
triage: contract-mismatch
status: open
feature: FastAPI route GET /api/semantic/health (feat_route_14b7feb076)

evidence:
- backend/src/api/routes/memory/semantic/router.py:87-93 (_build_semantic_service)
- backend/src/api/routes/memory/semantic/router.py:185-197 (health_check)

The semantic service used by summarize/title depends on both an LLM client and provider API-key loading, but the health route only checks that container.llm_client exists before returning a ready payload. That can make GET /api/semantic/health advertise the semantic summarization service as ready even when provider credentials/configuration required by the actual semantic operations are missing or unusable.

recommendation:
Align the health check with the readiness contract: either narrow the payload wording to say only the LLM client dependency is present, or validate the same semantic configuration needed by SemanticSummarizationService before returning ready.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for this route, and the shown file has no route-level assertion that readiness covers provider/API-key configuration.

suggested regression test:
Add a FastAPI route test where container.llm_client is present but provider key loading/configuration is unavailable, and assert /api/semantic/health does not report full semantic service readiness unless that is intentionally only a shallow dependency check.

minimum fix scope:
Update health_check readiness semantics and add a focused route test for shallow-versus-full readiness behavior.

## low: Settings handlers are defined but not exported from the handlers package

- backend/src/api/handlers/settings.py:55-67 (LoadSettingsHandler)
- backend/src/api/handlers/settings.py:148-160 (UpdateSettingsHandler)

The package-level handlers module exports ListModelsHandler from settings.py but omits LoadSettingsHandler and UpdateSettingsHandler even though both are concrete message handlers in the same source group. Code that imports handlers through backend.src.api.handlers cannot access the load/update settings handlers, which is an API surface mismatch and can lead to registration/import drift as settings behavior evolves.

recommendation:
Import LoadSettingsHandler and UpdateSettingsHandler in backend/src/api/handlers/__init__.py and include them in __all__, or document that only ListModelsHandler is intended to be exported and adjust package exports consistently.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for handler package exports or registry import coverage.

suggested regression test:
Add a small import/export test that asserts all concrete settings handlers intended for registry use are importable from backend.src.api.handlers.

minimum fix scope:
backend/src/api/handlers/__init__.py export list.

repro:
Attempt `from backend.src.api.handlers import LoadSettingsHandler, UpdateSettingsHandler`; the import fails because the package does not import or include those classes in __all__.

## low: Source-text assertion is cwd-dependent and can fail outside the repo root

id: fnd_sig-feat-test-suite-b56217e3a7-b_2b2cbfd16c
category: build-release
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python test suite tests/backend#1 (feat_test-suite_b56217e3a7)

evidence:
- tests/backend/test_api_container_source.py:16-18

The test opens backend/src/core/container/api_container.py through a relative Path without anchoring it to the test file or repository root. Running pytest from a different working directory, or through a test runner that changes cwd, will raise FileNotFoundError before checking the intended contract. This makes the suite less portable and can create false CI failures unrelated to the backend behavior being tested.

recommendation:
Resolve the source path relative to the repository root or the test file location, or replace the text search with an import-level behavioral assertion that does not depend on cwd.

test analysis:
The test itself is the fragile behavior; the included tests do not exercise running the suite from a non-root working directory.

suggested regression test:
Add a small test or helper assertion that resolves the target source path from pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve() and succeeds regardless of the current working directory.

minimum fix scope:
Update tests/backend/test_api_container_source.py to anchor the path resolution before read_text, or replace the source-text check with a behavior-based assertion.

## low: Streaming errors expose raw provider exception text to clients

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-ed619aedb2-4edd_c5e8d7a080
category: security
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Python source backend/src/llm/providers/:base (feat_library_ed619aedb2)

evidence:
- backend/src/llm/providers/base.py:237-247 (LLMProvider.get_completion_stream)
- backend/src/llm/providers/base.py:141-164 (LLMProvider._get_completion_with_standard_errors)

The non-streaming path maps provider/API failures to standard LLMAPIError/LLMError messages, but the streaming path yields ErrorEvent content built from str(e) for LiteLLM APIError and all unexpected exceptions. Provider exception strings can include upstream response bodies, request URLs, deployment names, or other operational details that should not be sent directly to the model/user stream. This also creates inconsistent public error contracts between streaming and non-streaming calls.

recommendation:
Use the same sanitized error mapping as the non-streaming path for stream ErrorEvent content, preserving detailed exception text in server logs only.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for streaming error content, and a test would need to assert that sensitive exception details are logged but not emitted in ErrorEvent.content.

suggested regression test:
Add a streaming test where _stream_internal raises an exception containing a fake secret or upstream body, then assert the yielded ErrorEvent contains only the sanitized provider error text.

minimum fix scope:
Change get_completion_stream exception handlers to emit sanitized messages using the existing status-code/error-message helpers and add focused tests for APIError and unexpected exceptions.

repro:
Raise a LiteLLM APIError or generic exception from _stream_internal whose message contains provider response details; get_completion_stream yields that raw message in ErrorEvent.content.

## low: Successful destructive IPC can be reported as a failed delete if the UI callback throws

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-1269f40ef6-ee01_1a47e4907b
category: bug
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/features/dashboard/components/sections/settings (feat_library_1269f40ef6)

evidence:
- frontend/src/renderer/features/dashboard/components/sections/settings/useMemorySettingsActions.js:34-56 (runDestructiveAction)
- frontend/src/renderer/features/dashboard/components/sections/settings/useMemorySettingsActions.js:67-73 (clearChatHistory)
- frontend/src/renderer/features/dashboard/components/sections/settings/MemorySettingsTab.jsx:41-43

runDestructiveAction treats the optional onSuccess callback as part of the destructive operation. For chat deletion, the IPC clear can succeed and already delete the data, but if onChatsCleared throws or rejects, the catch path changes the status to an error and returns false. That misreports the persisted result and can lead users to retry or distrust the state after data has already been removed.

recommendation:
Separate destructive IPC success from post-success UI refresh. Set the delete success state after a successful IPC response, then handle onSuccess errors independently with a refresh-specific warning or by logging them without changing the deletion result.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for MemorySettingsTab or useMemorySettingsActions, especially the partial-success path where IPC succeeds but the callback fails.

suggested regression test:
Mock CLEAR_CHAT_HISTORY to resolve successfully and pass an onChatsCleared callback that rejects; assert the hook reports the chat deletion as successful or a distinct refresh warning rather than a destructive-action failure.

minimum fix scope:
useMemorySettingsActions success/error ordering plus a hook or component regression test.

## low: Test shutdown leaves authentication and config state behind

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-cd310e97cf-534b_d11316188a
category: test-gap
confidence: medium
triage: test-gap
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/main/:ipc (feat_library_cd310e97cf)

evidence:
- frontend/src/main/ipc.cjs:131-155
- frontend/src/main/ipc.cjs:710-724

The IPC module keeps process-global state for install auth, user/session identity, active config, connection state, and runtime objects. The test shutdown helper resets the runtime, windows, observers, callbacks, and some pending state, but it does not clear currentUserId, currentInstallId, currentInstallToken, latestFrontendConfig, hasAttemptedInitialSettingsSync, or currentGlobalAgentStopShortcutStatus. Tests that initialize IPC after a prior authenticated/configured test can accidentally reuse stale auth headers or config values, masking regressions or making tests order-dependent.

recommendation:
Extend shutdownIpcForTests to reset all process-global IPC state that can influence a subsequent initialization, especially install auth, frontend config, shortcut status, and first-query/settings flags. Keep the reset list close to the declarations so future globals are harder to miss.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for this feature, and the existing shutdown helper itself is the only test isolation mechanism visible in the excerpt.

suggested regression test:
Add a Jest test that initializes IPC with auth/config state, calls shutdownIpcForTests, reinitializes, and asserts that subsequent handshakes/status payloads do not include the prior install token, user id, config, or shortcut status.

minimum fix scope:
frontend/src/main/ipc.cjs shutdownIpcForTests plus a focused frontend IPC test.

## low: User-controlled model name is written to logs without sanitization

The route accepts request.model_name from the network request and passes it directly into route log messages. If model_name can contain newlines or control characters, a caller can forge or obscure log entries, which matters because this route crosses network and user-input trust boundaries and logs both normal and failure paths.

minimum fix scope:
Sanitize or canonicalize request.model_name at the logging boundary in this router or in the logging helper functions.

## low: Visibility intent persistence can be lost after an interrupted write or corrupt JSON

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-d21c8fcd19-4b2b_d887201964
category: data-loss
confidence: medium
triage: risk
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/main/:chat (feat_library_d21c8fcd19)

evidence:
- frontend/src/main/chat_pill_visibility_intent_store.cjs:26-30 (readChatPillVisibilityIntent)
- frontend/src/main/chat_pill_visibility_intent_store.cjs:41-45 (writeChatPillVisibilityIntent)

The store writes the only persisted copy directly to the target JSON file and the reader treats any parse/read error as `{ userHidden: false }`. If the app exits, the process crashes, or the filesystem fails mid-write after truncation, the next startup silently interprets the corrupt or partial file as a visible chat pill. That loses the user's explicit hidden intent instead of preserving the prior value or failing closed for the persisted preference.

recommendation:
Write atomically through a temp file in the same directory followed by rename, and consider preserving the last known value or returning an error marker for corrupt state instead of silently resetting to visible.

test analysis:
No linked tests are included for this feature, and the shown package manifest only exposes generic Jest commands rather than a focused persistence/corruption regression.

suggested regression test:
Add a Jest test for the intent store that verifies writes are atomic or, at minimum, that corrupt existing state does not silently convert a previously hidden intent into visible without an explicit reset path.

minimum fix scope:
Update `writeChatPillVisibilityIntent` to use temp-file plus rename semantics and adjust `readChatPillVisibilityIntent` error handling/tests for corrupt persisted state.

repro:
Persist `{ userHidden: true }`, replace `chat-pill-visibility-intent.json` with a truncated JSON value such as `{ "userHidden": tru`, then call `readChatPillVisibilityIntent`; it returns `{ userHidden: false }`.

## low: Wakeword backend failures are silently fire-and-forgotten

id: fnd_sig-feat-library-f0fc56ef53-0ab9_6d293bd91c
category: api-contract
confidence: medium
triage: contract-mismatch
status: open
feature: Node source frontend/src/renderer/app/runtime#2 (feat_library_f0fc56ef53)

evidence:
- frontend/src/renderer/app/runtime/desktopVoiceRuntimeClient.ts:6-9

wakewordDetected starts the backend transport request and discards the returned promise without a catch or any caller-visible completion signal. If the SDK-hosted Electron backend rejects or is unavailable, the renderer cannot observe or handle the failure, and the runtime may produce an unhandled rejection depending on the transport implementation.

recommendation:
Either return the promise from wakewordDetected so callers can handle failure, or catch and route the error through the renderer's established runtime logging/error-reporting path.

test analysis:
No linked tests are provided for this feature, and there is no evidence of a contract test asserting how wakeword transport failures are surfaced.

suggested regression test:
Add a test that stubs wakewordDetected to reject and asserts the client either returns the rejection to the caller or logs/reports it through the chosen error channel.

minimum fix scope:
Change desktopVoiceRuntimeClient wakewordDetected error handling contract and cover the rejection path.

repro:
Make createDesktopBackendTransport(null).wakewordDetected reject, then call DesktopVoiceRuntimeClient.wakewordDetected and observe that the client neither reports failure nor handles the rejected promise locally.
